\displaystyle\left.+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}+R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}\rangle\right)\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}.

(2.14)

We can simplify this expression a lot. Indeed,

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w}=\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w},

(2.15)

while

\displaystyle e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}=\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}_{\vec{e}_{i}}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}.

(2.16)

Finally, we have

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle+\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\bigg\}\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\bigg\}\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}

\displaystyle=e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\left(2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\left(\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\left(e^{2\lambda}\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\left(e^{2\lambda}\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{\perp}\vec{w}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}.

(2.17)

Therefore, we have by (
2.2.2
), (
2.2.2
), (
2.16
), and (
2.2.2
)

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}_{\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t}}\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t}}\vec{w}_{t}\right)_{|t=0}=-2\,\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}\vec{e}_{i}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+\mathscr{R}_{i,j}^{0}(\vec{w},d\vec{w})

\displaystyle-e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}-e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}

\displaystyle+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}

\displaystyle=-2\,\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}_{\vec{e}_{i}}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{R}_{i,j}^{0}(\vec{w},d\vec{w}).

(2.18)

Finally, we have by (
2.2.2
) and (
2.2.2
)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\,\pi_{\vec{n}_{t}}\left(R(\vec{w}_{t},\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t}\right)=\nabla_{\vec{w}}R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j}+R(\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w})\vec{e}_{j}+R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}

\displaystyle-\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\left(R(\vec{w}_{t},\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\vec{w}}R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j}+R(\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w})\vec{e}_{j}+R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}-e^{-2\lambda}\langle R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}

\displaystyle-e^{-2\lambda}\langle R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}.

(2.19)

Thanks to (
2.2.2
), (
2.2.2
), and (
2.2.2
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})=-\nabla_{\nabla^{\top}_{\vec{e}_{i}}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}}^{N}\vec{w}-\bigg(-2\,\nabla^{N}_{\nabla_{\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}^{\top}\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}\vec{e}_{i}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}

\displaystyle+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\bigg)+\mathscr{R}_{i,j}(\vec{w},d\vec{w})

\displaystyle=-2\,\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}_{\vec{e}_{i}}\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}+2\,\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}_{\nabla^{\top}_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}-e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}-e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla^{N}_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}++\mathscr{R}_{i,j}(\vec{w},d\vec{w}).

(2.20)

for some curvature component
\mathscr{R}_{i,j}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle e^{-2\lambda}\left(\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})+\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\right)_{|t=0}=-2\,\nabla^{N}_{(\Delta_{g}\vec{w})^{\top}}\vec{w}+2\,\nabla^{N}_{\nabla^{\top}_{(\Delta_{g}\vec{\Phi})^{\top}}\vec{w}}\vec{w}

\displaystyle-\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}-\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}

\displaystyle=-2\,\nabla^{N}_{(\Delta_{g}\vec{w})^{\top}}\vec{w}-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}

(2.21)

since
2\vec{H}=\Delta_{g}\vec{\Phi}
is normal.

Now, recall that by (
2.3
), (
2.2.1
), and (
2.5
)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(g_{i,j}\right)_{|t=0}=\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left(g_{i,j}\right)_{|t=0}=2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}\rangle-2\langle R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j},\vec{w}\rangle

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\det(g_{t})_{|t=0}=2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}e^{4\lambda}

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\det(g_{t})_{|t=0}=2\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w},\vec{w})\right)e^{4\lambda}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{\det(g_{t})}

\displaystyle=-2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}e^{-4\lambda}

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\frac{1}{\det(g_{t})}

\displaystyle=-2\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w},\vec{w})\right)e^{-4\lambda}+8\,\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}e^{-4\lambda}

\displaystyle=-2\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-3\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w},\vec{w})\right)e^{-4\lambda}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}g^{i,j}=\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left((-1)^{i+j}\frac{g_{i+1,j+1}}{\det(g_{t})}\right)=2(-1)^{i+j}e^{-4\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w}\rangle-\langle R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i+1})\vec{e}_{j+1},\vec{w}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-4\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}e^{-4\lambda}(-1)^{i+j}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j+1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i+1}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-2\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-3\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w},\vec{w})\right)e^{-2\lambda}(-1)^{i+j}\delta_{i,j}.

Since

\displaystyle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})=\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{i},\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{i},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w})+\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j}\right),

we get

\displaystyle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}g^{i,j}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}=e^{-2\lambda}\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{\frac{d}{dt}}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{\perp}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})+\nabla^{\perp}_{\frac{d}{dt}}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{\perp}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\right)

\displaystyle-2\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{i},\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{i},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w})+\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\bigg\{2(-1)^{i+j}e^{-4\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w}\rangle-\langle R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i+1})\vec{e}_{j+1},\vec{w}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-4\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}e^{-4\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j+1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i+1}\rangle\right)\bigg\}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}

\displaystyle-2\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-3\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w},\vec{w})\right)e^{-2\lambda}\left(\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}\right).

(2.22)

Since
\vec{H}=\dfrac{1}{2}e^{-2\lambda}\left(\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}\right)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}g^{i,j}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}=e^{-2\lambda}\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{\frac{d}{dt}}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{\perp}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})+\nabla^{\perp}_{\frac{d}{dt}}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{\perp}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\right)

\displaystyle-2\,e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{i},\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{i},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w})+\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\bigg\{2(-1)^{i+j}e^{-4\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w}\rangle-\langle R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i+1})\vec{e}_{j+1},\vec{w}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-4\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}e^{-4\lambda}(-1)^{i+j}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j+1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i+1}\rangle\right)\bigg\}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}

\displaystyle-4\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-3\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w},\vec{w})\right)\vec{H}.

(2.23)

First compute

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle=\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}+\nabla_{\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{z}+\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\\
&\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle=\langle i(\nabla_{\vec{e}_{z}}-\nabla_{\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}})\vec{w},i(\vec{e}_{z}-\vec{e}_{\overline{z}})\rangle=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\\
&\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle=i\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}+\nabla_{\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{z}-\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\rangle+i\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}-\nabla_{\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{z}+\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\rangle\\
&=2\,i\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{z}\rangle-\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\rangle\right)=-4\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.24)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}=e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{z}+\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z}+\vec{e}_{\overline{z}})=\vec{H}+\,\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})\\
&e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}=\vec{H}-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{H}_{0}\right)\\
&e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}=-\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0}).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.25)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}(-1)^{i+j}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j+1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i+1}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}=\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}-4\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\vec{H}+\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})\right)+\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}+4\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\vec{H}-\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})\right)

\displaystyle-8\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0})

\displaystyle=2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\vec{H}-8\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})-8\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0})

\displaystyle=2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\vec{H}-8\,\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

(2.26)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}\rangle&=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\\
\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}\rangle&=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\\
\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}\rangle&=-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.27)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}(-1)^{i+j}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i+1}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j+1}}\vec{w}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}=\left(\frac{1}{2}|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\vec{H}+\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{2}|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\vec{H}-\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})\right)

\displaystyle-4\,\mathrm{Im}\,(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w})\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0})=|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}\vec{H}-4\,\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

(2.28)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}&=2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w}\right)+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}\right)\\
\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{2},\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}&=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}\right)\\
\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}+\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{2},\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}&=-4\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}\right),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.29)

which implies by (
2.24
) and (
2.29
) that

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{i},\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w}

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(e^{-2\lambda}\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+2\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(e^{-2\lambda}\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+16\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\mathrm{Im}\,\left(e^{-2\lambda}\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z}}\vec{w}\right)=\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+16\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

(2.30)

Since

\displaystyle\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{1},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{\top}\vec{w})=e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{2},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{\top}\vec{w})=e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}+e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}

Then, we compute by (
2.24
) and (
2.25
)

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{i},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w})=2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\left(e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}\right)+2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\left(e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}+e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-2\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\left(e^{-4\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}+e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+e^{-4\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle^{2}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\bigg\}e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle^{2}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\bigg\}e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle+\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\right)\bigg\}e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}.

(2.31)

Now, recall that by definition (
[
20
,
2.147
p.
115
]
), for all smooth functions
a,b:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}

\displaystyle\{a,b\}=\partial_{x_{1}}a\cdot\partial_{x_{2}}b-\partial_{x_{1}}a\cdot\partial_{x_{1}}b=\nabla^{\perp}a\cdot\nabla b,

(2.32)

where
\nabla^{\perp}=(-\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{1}})
. On the other hand, since
\partial_{z}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{x_{1}}-i\,\partial_{x_{2}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle-4\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial_{z}a\cdot\partial_{z}b\right)=-\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\left(\partial_{x_{1}}a-i\,\partial_{x_{2}}a\right)\cdot\left(\partial_{x_{1}}b-i\,\partial_{x_{2}}b\right)\right)=\partial_{x_{1}}a\cdot\partial_{x_{2}}b+\partial_{x_{2}}a\cdot\partial_{x_{2}}b.

(2.33)

Therefore, we get by (
2.32
) and (
2.33
)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{x_{1}}a\cdot\partial_{x_{2}}b=\frac{1}{2}\{a,b\}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial_{z}a\cdot\partial_{z}b\right)\\
&\partial_{x_{2}}a\cdot\partial_{x_{1}}b=-\frac{1}{2}\{a,b\}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial_{z}a\cdot\partial_{z}b\right).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.34)

Therefore, we deduce by (
2.34
) that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle=-\frac{1}{2}\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\\
&\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.35)

Therefore, we have by (
2.2.2
) and (
2.35
)

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle^{2}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\bigg\}e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle^{2}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\bigg\}e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}

\displaystyle+e^{-4\lambda}\bigg\{2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+2\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle+\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\right)\bigg\}e^{-2\lambda}\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{2\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-4\left(\frac{1}{2}\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\bigg\}\left(\vec{H}+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{H}_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\bigg\{2\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-4\left(-\frac{1}{2}\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\bigg\}\left(\vec{H}-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{H}_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\bigg\{2\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(-\frac{1}{2}\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+2\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-4\,\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\bigg\}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0})

\displaystyle=\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)\vec{H}

\displaystyle+\bigg\{8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)-4\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\bigg\}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})

\displaystyle+\bigg\{8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)+4\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\bigg\}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0})

\displaystyle=\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)\vec{H}+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\,\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-4\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}},

(2.36)

where we used the formula

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Re}\,(a)\cdot\mathrm{Im}\,(b)+\mathrm{Im}\,(a)\cdot\mathrm{Re}\,(b)=\frac{1}{4i}\Big\{-(a+\overline{a})\cdot(b-\overline{b})+(a-\overline{a})\cdot(b+\overline{b})\Big\}=\mathrm{Im}\,\left(a\cdot\overline{b}\right),

so that

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0})=\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-2}\otimes\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\otimes\overline{\vec{h}_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-2}\otimes\left(i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\otimes\overline{\vec{h}_{0}}\right)=\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

since
\mathrm{Im}\,(a)=-\,\mathrm{Re}\,(i\,a)
for all
a\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
. Therefore, we deduce by (
2.2.2
) and (
2.2.2
) that

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{i},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w})=\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)\vec{H}

\displaystyle+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\,\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-4\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

(2.37)

Therefore, we deduce by (
2.2.2
), (
2.2.2
), (
2.2.2
), (
2.2.2
), (
2.2.2
), and (
2.2.2
) that

\displaystyle 2\,\left(\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{H}_{g_{t}}\right)_{|t=0}=\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}g^{i,j}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\right)_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=-2\,\nabla^{N}_{(\Delta_{g}\vec{w})^{\top}}\vec{w}-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}

\displaystyle-2\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+16\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)

\displaystyle-2\left(\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)\vec{H}+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\,\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-4\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)

\displaystyle+2\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}\vec{H}-4\,\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)-4\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\left(2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\vec{H}-8\,\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)

\displaystyle-4\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-3\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)\vec{H}+\mathscr{R}_{2}(\vec{w},d\vec{w})

\displaystyle=-2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}-32\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-2\,\nabla^{N}_{(\Delta_{g}\vec{w})^{\top}}\vec{w}

\displaystyle-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{H}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{N}\vec{w}-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{N}\vec{w}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+16\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+\left(-2|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+32|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|^{2}_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)\vec{H}+\mathscr{R}_{2}(\vec{w},d\vec{w})

(2.38)

for some curvature operator
\mathscr{R}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle 2\langle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{H}_{g_{t}},\vec{H}_{g_{t}}\rangle_{|t=0}=-2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\left\langle\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w},\vec{H}\right\rangle-32\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle-2\langle\langle d\vec{\Phi},\Delta_{g}\vec{w}\rangle,\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\rangle_{g}-2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+16\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+\left(-2|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+32|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}+\langle\mathscr{R}_{2}(\vec{w},d\vec{w}),\vec{H}\rangle.

(2.39)

Since

\displaystyle 2\left(\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{H}_{g_{t}}\right)_{|t=0}=\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}g^{i,j}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\right)

\displaystyle=-e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}+e^{-2\lambda}\left(\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})+\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})\right)

\displaystyle=-e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}+e^{-2\lambda}\left(\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{1},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}^{\top}\vec{w}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{2}_{\vec{e}_{2},\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{2},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{\top}\vec{w})+\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1})\vec{e}_{1}+R\left(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2})\vec{e}_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}+\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}

\displaystyle+e^{-2\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,1}+\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2}+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,2}\right)+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w})

\displaystyle=\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}-\frac{1}{2}e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w})=\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w}),

(2.40)

where
\mathscr{A}
is the Simons operator, and where we used the identities:

\displaystyle g^{i,j}_{|t=0}=e^{-2\lambda}\delta_{i,j}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}g^{i,j}_{|t=0}=-e^{-4\lambda}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}_{t},\partial_{x_{j}}\vec{\Phi}_{t})_{|t=0}=\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i},\vec{e}_{j}}^{N}+\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{i},\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}^{\top}\vec{w})+\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(R(\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i})\vec{e}_{j}\right).

Notice that thanks to (
2.24
) and (
2.25
)

\displaystyle e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{i}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\vec{e}_{j}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{i}\rangle\right)\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j}=\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}+4\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\vec{H}+\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}-4\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\right)\left(\vec{H}-\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{H}_{0})\right)+8\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\right)\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{H}_{0})

\displaystyle=2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\vec{H}+8\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}},

which shows that

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})=-\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\vec{H}-4\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

(2.41)

Thanks to (
2.2.2
) and (
2.2.2
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}|\vec{H}_{g_{t}}|^{2}_{|t=0}=\left(2\left|\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{H}_{g_{t}}\right|^{2}+2\left\langle\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\nabla_{\frac{d}{dt}}^{N}\vec{H}_{g_{t}},\vec{H}_{g_{t}}\right\rangle\right)_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}-2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\left\langle\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w},\vec{H}\right\rangle-32\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle-2\langle\langle d\vec{\Phi},\Delta_{g}\vec{w}\rangle,\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\rangle_{g}-2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+16\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+\left(-2|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+32|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}+\langle\mathscr{R}_{2}(\vec{w},d\vec{w}),\vec{H}\rangle.

(2.42)

Therefore, we finally deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left(|\vec{H}_{g_{t}}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{t}}\right)_{|t=0}=\left(\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}|\vec{H}_{g_{t}}|^{2}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+2\,\frac{d}{dt}\left(|\vec{H}_{g_{t}}|^{2}\right)_{|t=0}\frac{d}{dt}\left(d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{t}}\right)_{|t=0}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left(d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{t}}\right)_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}-\cancel{2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle}-32\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle-2\langle\langle d\vec{\Phi},\Delta_{g}\vec{w}\rangle,\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\rangle_{g}-2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+16\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+\left(-2|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}+2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}+32|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}+\langle\mathscr{R}_{2}(\vec{w},d\vec{w}),\vec{H}\rangle

\displaystyle+2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\cancel{\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w}),\vec{H}\rangle+\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}\bigg\}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(2.43)

Thanks to (
2.41
), we deduce that

\displaystyle 2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\mathscr{A}(\vec{w}),\vec{H}\rangle=-2\langle\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}^{2}|\vec{H}|^{2}-8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}},

which shows that

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left(|\vec{H}_{g_{t}}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{t}}\right)_{|t=0}=\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}-32\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle-2\langle\langle d\vec{\Phi},\Delta_{g}\vec{w}\rangle,\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\rangle_{g}-2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\langle\mathscr{R}_{2}(\vec{w},d\vec{w})+2\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\,\mathscr{R}(\vec{w}),\vec{H}\rangle\bigg\}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(2.44)

Finally, we deduce the following result.

Theorem 2.1
.

Let
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow(M^{n},h)
be a Willmore immersion. Then, for all variation
\vec{w}:\Sigma\rightarrow TM^{m}
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w})=D^{2}W(\vec{\Phi})(\vec{w},\vec{w})=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})+\mathscr{R}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}-32\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle-2\langle\langle d\vec{\Phi},\Delta_{g}\vec{w}\rangle,\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\rangle_{g}-2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\langle\mathscr{R}_{3}(\vec{w},d\vec{w}),\vec{H}\rangle\bigg\}d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

(2.45)

where
\mathscr{R}_{3}
is a curvature operator such that

\displaystyle|\mathscr{R}_{3}(\vec{w},d\vec{w})|\leq C\left(|\nabla R||\vec{w}|^{2}+|R|\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}+|\vec{\mathbb{I}}|_{g}|\vec{w}|\right)|\vec{w}|\right)

for some universal constant
C<\infty
.

From now, on, we assume that
M^{n}=\mathbb{R}^{n}
equipped with its standard metric now. We see that it is not obvious that the second derivative corresponds to the index of a self-adjoint operator.
Notice that in conformal coordinates, there holds

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w}=\left(\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{w}-2(\partial_{z}\lambda)\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w}\right)dz^{2}=e^{2\lambda}\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\left(e^{-2\lambda}\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w}\right)dz^{2},

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\langle\langle\vec{H},\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w}\rangle,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\int_{\Sigma}\left\langle\left\langle\vec{H},\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\left(e^{-2\lambda}\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{w}\right)\right\rangle,\langle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi},\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w}\rangle\right\rangle|dz|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\int_{\Sigma}e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{w},\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{H}\rangle\langle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi},\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w}\rangle|dz|^{2}\right)-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\langle e^{-2\lambda}\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi},\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w}\rangle|dz|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\int_{\Sigma}\langle\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\langle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi},e^{-2\lambda}\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w}\rangle|dz|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\partial\vec{\Phi}\otimes\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\frac{1}{16}\int_{\Sigma}\langle\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle,\langle d\vec{\Phi},\Delta_{g}\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{g}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left(-32\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-2\langle\langle d\vec{\Phi},\Delta_{g}\vec{w}\rangle,\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\rangle_{g}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=

\displaystyle 32\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+4\int_{\Sigma}|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

and the second derivative becomes

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w})=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}+2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle+32\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\bigg\}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(2.46)

We deduce that the following simpler expression holds.

Theorem 2.2
.

Let
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a smooth Willmore immersion. Then, for all
\vec{w}\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w})=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}+2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle+32\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+8\,\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}_{g}\langle i\,\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\bigg\}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(2.47)

If
\vec{w}
is assume to be normal, then

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w})=\int_{\Sigma}\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}+2|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}-\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-16|\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle+32\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-8\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+8\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\bigg\}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(2.48)

Theorem 2.3
.

Let
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a branched Willmore immersion. Then, a variation
\vec{w}\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that
|d\vec{w}|_{g}\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{0}})
is admissible if and only if

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}\in L^{2}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}).

Furthermore, for all such variations, the formula (
2.2
) holds.

Proof.

Thanks to the analysis of
[
5
]
, at a branched point of order
m
, there exists a bounded function
\mu
such that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda}=e^{\mu}|z|^{m-1},

and there exists an integer
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\alpha\leq m-1
and
\vec{C_{0}}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
such that

\displaystyle\vec{H}=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\frac{\vec{C}_{0}}{z^{\alpha-1}}\right)+O(|z|^{1-\alpha}\log|z|).

Thanks to the Codazzi equation, assuming for simplicity that
\mu(0)=0
, we deduce that for some
\vec{A}_{1}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}

\displaystyle\vec{h}_{0}=-\frac{(\alpha-1)}{2m}\vec{C}_{0}\,z^{m-\alpha-1}\overline{z}^{m}+\vec{A}_{1}z^{m-1}+O(|z|^{m}\log|z|).

In particular, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{H},\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}e^{2\lambda}|\leq Ce^{\lambda}|\nabla\vec{H}|\leq\frac{C}{|z|}

which is integrable, while the other terms can all be trivially bounded but

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})\right|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Since the identity (
2.41
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\leq C\int_{\Sigma}|A|^{2}|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}<\infty,

the conclusion of the theorem follows.
∎

2.3
Formula for normal variations

For a normal variation, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}(\vec{w})=e^{-4\lambda}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},\vec{w}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{i,j},

and

\displaystyle\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}=-2\langle\vec{H},\vec{w}\rangle

\displaystyle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle

\displaystyle\{\vec{\Phi},\vec{w}\}=\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}\cdot\nabla\vec{w}=-\partial_{x_{2}}\vec{\Phi}\cdot\nabla_{\vec{e}_{1}}\vec{w}+\partial_{x_{1}}\vec{\Phi}\cdot\nabla_{\vec{e}_{2}}\vec{w}=\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{1,2},\vec{w}\rangle-\langle\vec{\mathbb{I}}_{2,1},\vec{w}\rangle=0.

Therefore, the second derivative becomes

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}+2\langle\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{H}+2\langle\langle\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle,\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+2\int_{\Sigma}|\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle|_{g}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\left(|d\vec{w}|_{g}^{2}-4|\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\vec{H},\vec{w}\rangle\langle\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle,\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

The first three components (and the fifth component) obviously induce a self-adjoint operator.
We have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\Sigma}e^{-2\lambda}\left\langle\left\langle\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\vec{w},\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{H}\right\rangle,\left\langle\overline{\vec{h}_{0}},\vec{w}\right\rangle\right\rangle|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\Sigma}e^{-2\lambda}\left\langle\left\langle\vec{w},\left(\nabla^{N}_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}-2(\partial_{z}\lambda)\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\right)\vec{H}\right\rangle,\left\langle\overline{\vec{h}_{0}},\vec{w}\right\rangle\right\rangle|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\Sigma}e^{-2\lambda}\left\langle\left\langle\vec{w},\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{H}\right\rangle,\left\langle\overline{\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{h}_{0}},\vec{w}\right\rangle\right\rangle|dz|^{2}-\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\Sigma}e^{-2\lambda}\left\langle\left\langle\vec{w},\nabla_{\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}}^{N}\vec{H}\right\rangle,\left\langle\overline{\vec{h}_{0}},\nabla_{\partial_{z}}^{N}\vec{w}\right\rangle\right\rangle|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Sigma}\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{H},\vec{w}\rangle,\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\int_{\Sigma}|\langle\overline{\partial}\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\int_{\Sigma}\langle\langle\partial^{N}\vec{H},\vec{w}\rangle,\overline{\partial}^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

where we used the Codazzi identity
\overline{\partial}^{N}\vec{h}_{0}=g\otimes\partial^{N}\vec{H}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\langle\langle\partial^{N}\vec{H},\vec{w}\rangle,\vec{h}_{0}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\overline{\partial}^{N}\vec{w}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\langle\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}\vec{H},\vec{w}\rangle,\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}|\langle\overline{\partial}\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Therefore, if we introduce the symmetric bilinear form
B_{\vec{\Phi}}
on normal function in
W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})
defined by

\displaystyle B_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{v},\vec{w})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\langle\mathscr{L}_{g}\vec{v},\mathscr{L}_{g}\vec{w}\rangle d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+2\int_{\Sigma}\langle\langle d\vec{v},\vec{H}\rangle,\langle d\vec{w},\vec{H}\rangle\rangle_{g}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\left(\langle d\vec{v},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}-4\langle\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{v}\rangle,\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)|\vec{H}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{v}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial^{N}\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{v}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial\vec{v}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle+4\int_{\Sigma}\langle\vec{H},\vec{v}\rangle\langle\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{w}\rangle,\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+4\int_{\Sigma}\langle\vec{H},\vec{w}\rangle\langle\langle\vec{h}_{0},\vec{v}\rangle,\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

(2.49)

then

\displaystyle B_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w},\vec{w})=Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w}),

(2.50)

and we deduce that provided that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g}=\mathscr{L}_{g}^{2}+4\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(\langle d(\,\cdot\,),\vec{H}\rangle\vec{H}\right)-2\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(|\vec{H}|^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)+8\langle\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle,\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}|\vec{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}^{N}\vec{H}\right)-16\,\langle\partial^{N}(\,\cdot\,)\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\partial(\,\cdot\,)\right)

\displaystyle+8\langle\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle,\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\vec{H}+8\langle\vec{H},\,\cdot\,\rangle\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-2}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\overline{\vec{h}_{0}}\rangle\vec{h}_{0}\right)

(2.51)

the following identity holds

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{w})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\langle\vec{w},\mathcal{L}_{g}\vec{w}\,\rangle d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(2.52)

Furthermore, thanks to (
2.3
) and (
2.50
), we deduce that
\mathcal{L}_{g}
is a self-adjoint operator. In codimension
1
, taking
\vec{w}=u\,\vec{n}
for some
u\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
, we get

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,\mathcal{L}_{g}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{g}^{2}+2\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(H^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\partial H\right)-16\langle\partial(\,\cdot\,)\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}

\displaystyle+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(H\,g^{-1}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\partial(\,\cdot\,)\right)+24|H|^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}.

(2.53)

We can simplify this formula to

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{g}^{2}+2\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(H^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\partial\left(H\,\cdot\,\right)\right)-16\langle\partial(\,\cdot\,)\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+24|\vec{H}|^{2}|\vec{h}_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}.

(2.54)

Using the formula
\mathscr{L}_{g}=\Delta_{g}+|A|^{2}
, we can also rewrite this operator as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{g}^{2}+|A|^{2}\Delta_{g}+2\langle d|A|^{2},d(\,\cdot)\rangle_{g}+2\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(H^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)+\left(|A|^{4}+\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}+24|H|^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\partial\left(H\,\cdot\,\right)\right)-16\langle\partial(\,\cdot\,)\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

(2.55)

2.4
The Definition of the Morse Index

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\Sigma
be a closed Riemann surface, and
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a branched Willmore immersion. Define the following Sobolev space

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(\vec{\Phi})=W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\left\{\vec{w}:|d\vec{w}|_{g}\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma),\;\,\mathscr{L}_{g}\vec{w}\in L^{2}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g})\right\}.

Define the index of
\vec{\Phi}
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi})=\sup\left\{\dim(V):V\subset\mathscr{E}(\vec{\Phi})\;\,\text{such that}\;\,Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{v})<0\;\,\text{for all}\;\,\vec{v}\in V\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right\},

and the nullity of
\vec{\Phi}
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi})=\sup\left\{\dim(V):V\subset\mathscr{E}(\vec{\Phi})\;\,\text{such that}\;\,{Q_{\vec{\Phi}}}_{|V}=0\right\}.

Finally, the space of normal variations is defined by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}^{N}(\vec{\Phi})=\mathscr{E}(\vec{\Phi})\cap\left\{\vec{w}:\langle\vec{w},d\vec{\Phi}\rangle=0\right\}.

Remark 2.5
.

Thanks to Theorem
2.3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(\vec{\Phi})=W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\left\{\vec{w}:|d\vec{w}|_{g}\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma),\;\,\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}\in L^{2}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g})\right\}.

Furthermore, notice that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}^{N}\vec{w}=\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w}-\nabla^{\top}_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w}=\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w}-2e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\rangle\vec{e}_{z}-2e^{-2\lambda}\langle\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{z}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}^{N}\vec{w}

\displaystyle=4\,e^{-2\lambda}\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\nabla_{\partial_{z}}^{N}\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}^{N}\vec{w}\right)=(\Delta_{g}\vec{w})^{N}-8\,e^{-4\lambda}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{z})+\langle\nabla_{\partial_{\overline{z}}}\vec{w},\vec{e}_{z}\rangle\vec{\mathbb{I}}(\vec{e}_{z},\vec{e}_{\overline{z}})\right)

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{g}\vec{w})^{N}-4\langle\partial\vec{\Phi}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}-\langle d\vec{\Phi},d\vec{w}\rangle_{g}\vec{H},

which shows that

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(\vec{\Phi})=W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\left\{\vec{w}:|d\vec{w}|_{g}\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma),\;\,(\Delta_{g}\vec{w})^{N}\in L^{2}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g})\right\}.

Theorem 2.6
.

Let
\Sigma
be a closed Riemann surface, and
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a smooth Willmore immersion,
g=\vec{\Phi}^{\ast}g_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g}=\mathscr{L}_{g}^{2}+4\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(\langle d(\,\cdot\,),\vec{H}\rangle\vec{H}\right)-2\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(|\vec{H}|^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)+8\langle\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle,\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}|\vec{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}^{N}\vec{H}\right)-16\,\langle\partial^{N}(\,\cdot\,)\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial^{N}\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\partial(\,\cdot\,)\right)

\displaystyle+8\langle\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle,\langle\vec{h}_{0},\,\cdot\,\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\vec{H}+8\langle\vec{H},\,\cdot\,\rangle\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-2}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\overline{\vec{h}_{0}}\rangle\vec{h}_{0}\right)

be the operator acting on normal variations
\vec{w}\in\mathrm{Var}^{N}(\vec{\Phi})
. For all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, denote by
\mathscr{E}(\lambda)\subset\mathrm{Var}^{N}(\vec{\Phi})\subset W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})
the space of eigenfunctions associated to
\lambda
. Then, we define the index of
\vec{\Phi}
relative to
\mathcal{L}
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})=\dim\bigoplus_{\lambda<0}\mathscr{E}(\lambda),

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Null}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})=\mathrm{Ker}(\mathscr{L}).

Then, the following identities hold true:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi})&=\mathrm{Ind}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})<\infty\\
\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi})&=\mathrm{Null}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})<\infty.\end{aligned}\right.

(2.56)

Proof.

Case 1: The Codimension
1
Case.

Step 1: Spectral decomposition of
\mathcal{L}
.

Let
f\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
and consider the following equation on

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u=f.

Consider for all
\lambda>0
the minimisation problem

\displaystyle\inf\left\{Q_{\lambda}(u)=Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)+\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}f\,u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}:u\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)\right\}.

(2.57)

We have

\displaystyle\partial\vec{w}=\partial(u\,\vec{n})=\partial u\,\vec{n}+u\,\partial_{z}\vec{n}\,dz,

which shows that

\displaystyle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}=(\partial u)^{2}+u^{2}\langle\partial_{z}\vec{n},\partial_{z}\vec{n}\rangle dz^{2}.

We have

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\vec{n}=2e^{-2\lambda}\langle\partial_{z}\vec{n},\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\rangle\vec{e}_{z}+2e^{-2\lambda}\langle\partial_{z}\vec{n},\vec{e}_{z}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}=-H\,\vec{e}_{z}-H_{0}\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}

so that

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{z}\vec{n},\partial_{z}\vec{n}\rangle=e^{2\lambda}H\,H_{0},

and

\displaystyle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w}=(\partial u)^{2}+u^{2}\,H\,h_{0}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial\vec{w}\,\dot{\otimes}\,\partial\vec{w},\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}H^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g}u+|A|^{2}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+2\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g}^{2}H^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du|_{g}^{2}-4|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}u^{2}\right)H^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-8\int_{\Sigma}H^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle+8\int_{\Sigma}H^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u+|A|^{2}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du|_{g}^{2}+4|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}u^{2}\right)H^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Using the inequality

\displaystyle(a+b)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}a^{2}-b^{2},

we deduce that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du|_{g}^{2}H^{2}+\left(4H^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}-|A|^{4}\right)u^{2}\right)\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du|_{g}^{2}H^{2}+\left(4H^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}-|A|^{4}\right)u^{2}\right)\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-2\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g}^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}|H|d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-2\int_{\Sigma}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-2\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g}^{2}H^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-2\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g}^{2}|A|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-2\int_{\Sigma}|A|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

(2.58)

We have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g}^{2}|A|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Sigma}u\,\Delta_{g}u\,|A|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\int_{\Sigma}u\langle du,d|A|^{2}\rangle_{g}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=-\int_{\Sigma}u\,\Delta_{g}u\,|A|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}\,u^{2}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+4\int_{\Sigma}|A|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}\,u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

(2.59)

while

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}ud\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}e^{-2\lambda}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}(u^{2})\partial_{z}H\overline{h_{0}}=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\left(\partial_{z}(e^{-2\lambda}\partial_{z}H)\overline{h_{0}}+|\partial_{z}H|^{2}\right)|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\left(\langle\nabla_{\partial,\partial}^{2}H,h_{0}\rangle_{WP}+\frac{1}{4}|dH|_{g}^{2}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

where we used the Codazzi identity
\overline{\partial}h_{0}=g\otimes\partial H
.
By (
2.4
) and (
2.4
), we get

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)\geq\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\int_{\Sigma}(-8\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}H,h_{0}\rangle_{WP}+\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}+10|A|^{4})u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(2.60)

Since
\vec{\Phi}
is smooth, we deduce that
-8\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}H,h_{0}\rangle_{WP}+\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}+10|A|^{4}\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma)
, which implies that for

\displaystyle\lambda>2\left\|-8\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}H,h_{0}\rangle_{WP}+\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}+10|A|^{4}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)},

the bilinear form is coercive

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}(u,v)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\mathcal{L}_{g}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,v\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

and we deduce by the Lax-Milgram theorem that the minimisation problem (
2.57
) admits a unique solution
u
. Furthermore, thanks to the Rellich-Kondrachov theorem, if
\left\{u_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a minimising sequence, then
u_{k}\hookrightarrow u
weakly as
k\rightarrow\infty
, which shows that for all
v\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}v\,\mathcal{L}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\lambda\int_{\Sigma}u\,v\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\int_{\Sigma}f\,v\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=0,

(2.61)

which shows that
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}u=\mathcal{L}u+\lambda u=f
.

Furthermore, the previous analysis shows that for all
\lambda>4C_{1}^{2}+2C_{2}
, the following set

\displaystyle L^{2}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g})\cap\left\{u:Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)+\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\leq 1\right\}

is a compact subset of
L^{2}(\Sigma)
. Therefore,
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{-1}
is a compact operator, which shows that there exists a sequence
\left\{\mu_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty)
such that
\mu_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}0
and
\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})=\left\{0\right\}\cup\left\{\mu_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
(
[
8
, Théorème VI.
8
]
) Furthermore, there exists a Hilbert basis
\left\{e_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of
L^{2}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g})
made of eigenvectors of
\mathcal{L}
(
[
8
, Théorème VI.
11
]
) Finally, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
u_{k}\in L^{2}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g})
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{-1}u_{k}=\mu_{k}\,u_{k},

or

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u_{k}=\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{k}}-\lambda\right)u_{k}.

Notice that
u_{k}\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
by the previous elliptic regularity analysis.
Therefore, if
\lambda_{k}=\mu_{k}^{-1}-\lambda
, we deduce that
\sigma(\mathcal{L})=\left\{\lambda_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
. Furthermore, since
\left\{e_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are eigenvectors of
\mathcal{L}
, we deduce that there exists a Hilbertian base of eigenvectors of
\mathcal{L}
. Since
\lambda_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\infty
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})=\dim\bigoplus_{\lambda\leq 0}\mathcal{E}(\lambda)<\infty.

Step 2: First inequality.

If
\displaystyle V=\bigoplus_{\lambda\leq 0}\mathcal{E}(\lambda)
, then (up to relabelling) there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\left\{e_{k}\right\}_{k\geq N}
is a Hilbertian base of
V
. In particular, for all and
u\in V^{\perp}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
, we have the following expansion in
l^{2}(\mathbb{N})

\displaystyle u=\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\langle u,e_{k}\rangle e_{k},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u=\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\lambda_{\varphi(k)}\langle u,e_{k}\rangle e_{k},

where
\varphi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
\lambda_{\varphi(k)}>0
for all
k\geq N
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,\mathcal{L}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{\varphi(k)}\langle u,e_{k}\rangle^{2}>0

since
\lambda_{k}>0
for all
k\geq N
, and

\displaystyle\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\langle u,e_{k}\rangle^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\langle u,e_{k}\rangle^{2}=\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}>0.

Therefore, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi})&\leq\mathrm{Ind}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})<\infty\\
\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi})&\leq\mathrm{Null}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})<\infty.\end{aligned}\right.

(2.62)

Step 3: Second inequality.

Now, for all
\lambda<0
and
u\in\mathcal{E}(\lambda)
, we trivially have

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)=\lambda\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}<0,

while
Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)=0
for all
u\in\mathcal{E}(0)
.
Therefore, the following inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathrm{Ind}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})&\leq\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi})\\
\mathrm{Null}_{W}^{\mathcal{L}}(\vec{\Phi})&\leq\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.63)

Therefore, we have equality in (
2.62
), which completes the proof of the theorem in the case
n=3
.

Case 2: General Codimension
n-2\geq 1
.
We can identify
\mathrm{Var}^{N}(\vec{\Phi})
to
W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n-2})
with the help of a locally controlled trivialisation
\vec{n}_{1},\cdots,\vec{n}_{n-2}
of
\vec{n}
. Indeed, for all
p\in\Sigma
, there exists
r=r(p)>0
and
\vec{n}_{1}(p),\cdots,\vec{n}_{n-2}(p):B(p,r)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}(p)\wedge\cdots\wedge\vec{n}_{n-2}(p)\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(p,r),

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{j}(p)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(p,r))}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(p,r))}

where
C<\infty
is independent of
p
. Therefore, by compactness of
\vec{n}
, using a partition of unity subordinated to
(p,r(p))
, there exists a finite number
N<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\vec{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\rho_{j}\vec{n}_{1}(p_{1})\wedge\cdot\wedge\vec{n}_{n-2}(p_{n})

where
\rho_{j}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
. As it will be apparent in computations, it is not restrictive to assume that
N=1
. Furthermore, to make computations easier, assume that
n=4
. Then,
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}
, where
\langle\vec{n}_{1},\vec{n}_{2}\rangle=0
and

\displaystyle\left\|d\vec{n}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Sigma)}+\left\|d\vec{n}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Sigma)}\leq C\left\|d\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Sigma)}.

If
\vec{w}
is a normal variation, there exists
u_{1},u_{2}\in W^{1,\infty}(\Sigma)
such that

\displaystyle\vec{w}=u_{1}\,\vec{n}_{1}+u_{2}\,\vec{n}_{2}.

We have

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}\vec{w}=\Delta_{g}u_{1}\,\vec{n}_{1}+\Delta_{g}u_{2}\,\vec{n}_{2}+2\langle du_{1},d\vec{n}_{1}\rangle_{g}+2\langle du_{2},d\vec{n}_{2}\rangle_{g}+u_{1}\,\Delta_{g}\vec{n}_{1}+u_{2}\,\Delta_{g}\vec{n}_{2}.

Recalling that

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}\vec{n}+|d\vec{n}|_{g}^{2}\vec{n}=8\,g^{-1}\otimes\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\star\left(\overline{\partial}\vec{H}\wedge\partial\vec{\Phi}\right)\right)+2\,i\,g^{-2}\otimes\left(\overline{\vec{h}_{0}}\wedge\vec{h}_{0}\right),

we deduce that

\displaystyle|\Delta_{g}\vec{n}_{i}|\leq 2|d\vec{n}_{g}|^{2}+2|d\vec{H}|_{g}^{2},

which shows by smoothness of
\vec{\Phi}
that
\Delta_{g}^{\perp}\vec{w}\in L^{2}(\Sigma,g)
if and only
u=(u_{1},u_{2})\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|\mathscr{L}_{g}\vec{w}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}|\Delta_{g}u|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-C\int_{\Sigma}\left(|A|^{4}+|d\vec{H}|_{g}^{2}\right)|u|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

and we can apply the preceding proof to complete the proof of the theorem in the general setting.
∎

Remark 2.7
.

We will study the branched case in
[
45
]
. However, an arbitrary
W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
variation will not necessarily give an
admissible
variation for which the integral

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,\mathcal{L}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

is finite, since
H\notin L^{4}(\Sigma,d\mathrm{vol}_{g})
in general.

At this point, the reader must be weary and not want to hear about Willmore surfaces for a few hours. Let us therefore move to the eigenvalue estimates of some elegant fourth-order differential operators.

3
Refined Pointwise Estimate on the Second Fundamental Form

3.1
General Lemmas

We will need some lemmas from the appendix of
[
31
]
. First, we have the following result.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
0<a\leq 2\,b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
and
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega)
be a harmonic function, and assume that for some
a\leq r\leq b
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\partial_{\nu}u\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}=0.

Then, we have for all
z\in\Omega

\displaystyle|\nabla u(z)|\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\left(\frac{b}{b^{2}-|z|^{2}}+\frac{a}{|z|^{2}-a^{2}}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(3.1)

Proof.

Let
d\in\mathbb{R}
and
\left\{a_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that

\displaystyle u(z)=d\,\log|z|+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}z^{k}\right).

By assumption, we have
d=0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla u|^{2}=4|\partial_{z}u|^{2}=\left|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ast}}ka_{k}z^{k-1}\right|^{2}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}dx=2\pi\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ast}}k^{2}|a_{k}|^{2}\int_{a}^{b}\rho^{2k-1}d\rho=\pi\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ast}}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left|b^{2k}-a^{2k}\right|.

(3.2)

Then, we get by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
