\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)a_{k+1}z^{k}\right|\leq\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)|a_{k+1}|^{2}b^{2k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{2k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{2}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)|a_{k}|^{2}b^{2k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{b^{2}-|z|^{2}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\frac{1}{b}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\frac{b}{b^{2}-|z|^{2}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

where we used that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\geq\pi\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)|a_{k+1}|^{2}b^{2k+2}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2k+2}\right)\geq\frac{3\pi}{4}b^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)|a_{k+1}|^{2}b^{2k},

(3.3)

where the second inequality comes from the hypothesis
\left(\dfrac{a}{b}\right)^{2}\leq\dfrac{1}{4}
.
Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}-(k+1)a_{-(k+1)}\frac{1}{z^{k+2}}\right|\leq\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)|a_{-(k+1)}|^{2}\frac{1}{a^{2k+2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)\frac{a^{2k+2}}{|z|^{2k+4}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{|z|^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{2}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)|a_{-(k+1)}|^{2}\frac{1}{a^{2k+2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\frac{a}{|z|^{2}-a^{2}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

using a similar inequality to (
3.3
).
Finally, we get the estimate

\displaystyle|\nabla u(z)|\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\left(\frac{b}{b^{2}-|z|^{2}}+\frac{a}{|z|^{2}-a^{2}}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

This inequality is very similar to the following inequality for holomorphic functions
\varphi:\mathbb{D}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathbb{D}}\,(1-|z|^{2})|\varphi(z)|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{D})}

that is proved in a similar fashion.

Corollary 3.3
.

Let
0<a<16\,b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and let
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega)
be a harmonic function such that for all some
a\leq r\leq b
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\partial_{\nu}u\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}=0.

Then, we have for all
4a\leq|z|\leq\dfrac{b}{4}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}(0)}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}\left(\frac{|z|}{b}+\frac{a}{|z|}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.4)

Proof.

Since
4\,a\leq|z|\leq\dfrac{b}{4}
for all
w\in B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}(0)
, we deduce by the point-wise bound (
3.1
) from Lemma
3.1
that

\displaystyle|\nabla u(w)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\left(\frac{b}{b^{2}-|w|^{2}}+\frac{a}{|w|^{2}-a^{2}}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\left(\frac{b}{b^{2}\left(1-\left(\frac{|w|}{b}\right)^{2}\right)}+\frac{a}{|w|^{2}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{|w|}\right)^{2}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{3\sqrt{3\pi}}\left(\frac{1}{b}+\frac{a}{|w|^{2}}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Therefore, we deduce by Minkowski’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{3\sqrt{3\pi}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{b}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}(0))}+\left\|\frac{a}{|w|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}(0))}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{3\sqrt{3\pi}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{b^{2}}\left(4|z|^{2}-\frac{|z|^{2}}{4}\right)}+\sqrt{\pi\,a^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\left(\frac{|z|}{2}\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(2|z|)^{2}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{3\sqrt{3\pi}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\sqrt{\frac{15\pi}{2}}\left(\frac{|z|}{b}+\frac{a}{|z|}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}\left(\frac{|z|}{b}+\frac{a}{|z|}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

which concludes the proof of the Corollary.
∎

We will now use the following elementary lemma that would follow from the mean-value formula in the case of balls without the extra hypothesis (
3.5
) on the flux.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
0<t<1
let
\Omega_{t}=B_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}a}(0)
. Let
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega)
be a harmonic function such that for some
a\leq r\leq b

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\partial_{\nu}u\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}=0.

(3.5)

Then, for all
\left(\dfrac{a}{b}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<t\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\leq t\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.6)

Proof.

Thanks to the proof of Lemma
3.1
and the hypothesis (
3.5
), there exists
\left\{a_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\mathbb{C}
such that for all
\left(\dfrac{a}{b}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<t\leq 1

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{t}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx=\pi\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ast}}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left|(t\,b)^{2k}-(t^{-1}a)^{2k}\right|.

(3.7)

We directly estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left|(t\,b)^{2k}-(t^{-1}a)^{2k}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left((t\,b)^{2k}-(t^{-1}a)^{2k}\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{k}|^{2}t^{2k}(b^{2k}-t^{-2k}a^{2k})

\displaystyle\leq t^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left(b^{2k}-a^{2k}\right)

(3.8)

since
t^{-2}\geq 1
, so that
-t^{-2k}a^{2k}\leq-a^{2k}
for all
k\geq 1
.
Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{-k}|^{2}\left|\frac{1}{(tb)^{2k}}-\frac{1}{(t^{-1}a)^{2k}}\right|=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{-k}|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{t}{a}\right)^{2k}-\left(\frac{t^{-1}}{b}\right)^{2k}\right)\leq t^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{-k}|^{2}\left(\frac{1}{a^{2k}}-\frac{1}{b^{2k}}\right).

(3.9)

Gathering the estimates (
3.1
) and (
3.9
) shows thanks to the identity (
3.6
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{t}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\leq t^{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}dx,

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Finally, recall the following lemma from
[
46
]
(see also
[
25
]
).

Lemma 3.5

(Lemma
2.3
,
[
46
]
)
.

Let
0<a<4\,b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
0<t<1
, let
\Omega_{t}=B_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}a}(0)
. Let
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega)
be a harmonic function such that for some
a\leq r\leq b

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\partial_{\nu}u\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}=0.

Then, for all
\left(\dfrac{a}{b}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<t<1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{t})}\leq 64\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{15}}\frac{t}{1-t}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.10)

The following extention lemma was used in
[
6
,
26
,
46
]
and a less precise version stated in
[
46
, Lemma
7.2
]
.

Lemma 3.6

(Lemma C.
1
,
[
31
]
)
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
0<(1+\varepsilon)r<R
,
\Omega=B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)
. Then, there exists a universal constant
C_{0}(\varepsilon)<\infty
depending only on
\varepsilon>0
with the following property. For all
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega)
, there exists an extension
\widetilde{u}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{C})
of
u
such that
\mathrm{supp}(\nabla\widetilde{u})\subset B_{(1+\varepsilon)R}\setminus\overline{B}_{(1+\varepsilon)^{-1}r}(0)
and satisfying the estimates

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \int_{\mathbb{C}}|\nabla\widetilde{u}|^{2}dx&\leq C_{0}(\varepsilon)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}dx.\\
\int_{B_{(1+\varepsilon)R}\setminus\overline{B}_{(1+\varepsilon)^{-1}R}(0)}|\nabla\widetilde{u}|^{2}dx&\leq C_{0}(\varepsilon)\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{(1+\varepsilon)^{-1}R}(0)}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\\
\int_{B_{(1+\varepsilon)r}\setminus\overline{B}_{(1+\varepsilon)^{-1}r}(0)}|\nabla\widetilde{u}|^{2}dx&\leq C_{0}(\varepsilon)\int_{B_{(1+\varepsilon)r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\end{aligned}\right.

(3.11)

We finish this section with an elementary result.

Theorem 3.7
.

There exists a constant
C_{W(2,1)}<\infty
with the following property. For all
0<R<\infty
, and for all
a,b\in W^{1,2}(\Omega)
, the solution of the following system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta u&=\nabla^{\perp}a\cdot\nabla b\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,R)\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,R)\end{aligned}\right.

(3.12)

satisfies the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,R))}\leq C_{W(2,1)}\left\|\nabla a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,R))}\left\|\nabla b\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,R))}.

(3.13)

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{u}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\widetilde{u}(x)=u(Rx)
for all
x\in B(0,1)
. Then,
\widetilde{u}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\widetilde{u}&=\nabla^{\perp}\widetilde{a}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{b}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\widetilde{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1),\end{aligned}\right.

(3.14)

where
\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1))
are defined similarly to
\widetilde{u}
.
Therefore, the classical (
[
20
, Théorème (
3.4.1
)]
) improved Wente estimate shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{W(2,1)}\left\|\nabla\widetilde{a}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|\nabla\widetilde{b}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

(3.15)

We have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\widetilde{a}|^{2}dx=\int_{B(0,1)}R^{2}|\nabla a(Rx)|^{2}dx=\int_{B(0,R)}|\nabla a|^{2}dx,\\
&\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\widetilde{b}|^{2}dx=\int_{B(0,R)}|\nabla b|^{2}dx.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.16)

Finally, for all
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{2}\left(B(0,1)\cap\left\{x:|\nabla\widetilde{u}(x)|>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,1)}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{|\nabla u(Rx)|>R^{-1}t\right\}}dx=\frac{1}{R^{2}}\int_{B(0,R)}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{|\nabla u(y)|>R^{-1}t\right\}}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{R^{2}}\mathscr{L}^{2}\left(B(0,R)\cap\left\{x:|\nabla u(x)|>R^{-1}t\right\}\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle=4\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{L}^{2}\left(B(0,1)\cap\left\{x:|\nabla\widetilde{u}(x)|>t\right\}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dt

\displaystyle=4\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{L}^{2}\left(B(0,R)\cap\left\{x:|\nabla u(x)|>R^{-1}t\right\}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{-1}\,dt

\displaystyle=4\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\mathscr{L}^{2}\left(B(0,R)\cap\left\{x:|\nabla u(x)|>s\right\}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}ds=\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,R))}.

(3.17)

Finally, the estimate follows from (
3.15
), (
3.16
), and (
3.1
).
∎

3.2
Improved Epsilon-Regularity for the Mean Curvature

Theorem 3.8
.

Let
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a conformal weak Willmore immersion. There exists a constant
\varepsilon_{0}>0
with the following property. Let
\vec{n}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the unit normal of the immersion
\vec{\Phi}
. Assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0}.

(3.18)

Then, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a positive constant
C_{k}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\nabla^{k}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\leq C_{k}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

(3.19)

Remark 3.9
.

The classical
\varepsilon
-regularity shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{k}\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\leq C_{k}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

A similar result is proved by Nicolas Marque in
[
33
]
by assuming only a smallness of the
L^{2}
norm of the mean curvature, but the trade-off is that the result is less general for the improved regularity only holds for
k=0
. A similar result was recently proved assuming a smallness of the trace-free second fundamental form by Bernard-Laurain-Marque, see
[
4
]
. The precised
\varepsilon
-regularity presented here is well-adapted to analysis related to the quantization of energy.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
[
55
]
by following the estimates more carefully. The following estimates hold for any Willmore immersion
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
(see
[
55
, (III.
13
,
20
,
21
)]
)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\leq C\left(\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx\right)

(3.20)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{4}))}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}

(3.21)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,\frac{1}{8}))}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{4}))}\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{4}))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{4}))}.

(3.22)

Therefore, adding all estimates (
3.20
), (
3.21
), and (
3.22
), we find that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,\frac{1}{8}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{4}))}\left(C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\right)

\displaystyle+\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{4}))}\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{2})}\left\|\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

Using the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,(2,1)}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow C^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{8}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,\frac{1}{8}))}+\left\|\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{8}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{2})}\left\|\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}+C\left\|\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\frac{1}{8}))}.

(3.23)

Of course, one can replace the
\dfrac{1}{8}
by
\dfrac{1}{2}
up to slightly modifying the cutoff functions, and using the Harnack inequality on the conformal parameter, we finally obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

(3.24)

Furthermore, since
\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}}
, we obtain the announced bound for a universal constant. As in
[
55
]
, this last estimate can be bootstrapped and gives the announced series of inequalities.
∎

Let us now precise
[
6
, Lemma VI.
1
]
and its counterpart
[
46
, Theorem
4.2
]
.

Theorem 3.10
.

There exists a constant
\varepsilon_{1}(n)>0
with the following property. Let
0<2\,a\leq b<\infty
and
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,b)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a weak conformal Willmore immersion such that

\displaystyle\sup_{a<r<b/2}\int_{B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{1}(n).

(3.25)

Let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<t\leq 1
, define
\Omega_{t}=B_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}a}(0)
. Let

\displaystyle E=\left\|\nabla\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Then, the exists a universal constant
C_{1}(n)<\infty
such that for all
\left(\dfrac{r}{R}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<t\leq\dfrac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\|\nabla^{2}S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C_{1}e^{C_{1}E}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C_{1}e^{C_{1}E}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.26)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\|\nabla^{2}S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C_{1}e^{C_{1}E}\left(\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\\
&\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C_{1}e^{C_{1}E}\left(\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right).\end{aligned}\right.

(3.27)

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{t})}+\left\|e^{\lambda}\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C_{1}e^{C_{1}E}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.28)

Remark 3.11
.

The results are stated for
0<8\,a<b
in
[
6
]
and
0<100\,a<b
in
[
46
]
, but those are just artifacts of the proof, and we need only assume that the conformal class
\log\left(\dfrac{R}{r}\right)
is bounded away from
-\infty
. The bound on
R
from
[
46
]
is not necessary either but convenient to apply extension results.

Proof.

We need only show the proof for
t=\dfrac{1}{2}
. For all
R>1
and
z\in\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}}
, define

\displaystyle A(R,z)=B_{R|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{R^{-1}|z|}(0).

(3.29)

Thanks to the Harnack inequality on the conformal parameter in dyadic annuli of the neck region (
[
6
, Lemma V.
2
]
) that we can apply here, we deduce that for all
z\in\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}}
the estimate

\displaystyle e^{\lambda(z)}|\vec{H}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left({\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int}_{\mkern-10.0muA(2,z)}e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(1+\varepsilon_{1})\varepsilon_{1}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}=C_{0}^{\prime}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(3.30)

Likewise, we have

\displaystyle e^{\lambda(z)}|\nabla\vec{H}(z)|\leq\frac{C_{1}^{\prime}}{|z|}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(3.31)

Therefore, if we define for all
0<r<\dfrac{b}{2}

\displaystyle\delta(r)=\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{A(2,r)}e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

where
A(2,r)=B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{r}{2}}(0)
, then we can replace
\delta
as defined in the equation preceding (VI.
9
) in
[
6
]
(and also in the proof of the
L^{2,1}
quantization in
[
46
, Theorem
4.2
, equation preceding (
4.6
)]
) by this new
\delta
function, up to a few modifications that we will indicate now. Rather than detailing those changes in
[
6
]
, we will directly show them for
[
46
]
.

We will also need the classical
\varepsilon
-regularity for
\vec{n}
, that states that for all
z\in\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla\vec{n}(z)|\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(3.32)

Then, recall that the Willmore equation implies that there exists
\vec{L}:B_{b}(0)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}

\displaystyle 2i\,\partial\vec{L}=\partial\vec{H}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial\vec{\Phi}+g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle{\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}}.

(3.33)

Indeed, the imaginary part of the right-hand side is closed (this is the weak Willmore equation first proved in
[
55
]
), so the result follows from the Poincaré lemma. Notice that in real coordinatres, we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}\vec{L}=\nabla\vec{H}-3\nabla^{N}\vec{H}+\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right).

(3.34)

By virtue of (
3.2
) and (
3.32
), we directly from either (
3.33
) or (
3.34
)

\displaystyle e^{\lambda}|\nabla\vec{L}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{|z|}\delta(|z|)+\delta^{2}(|z|)+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\delta(|z|)\right)\leq\frac{C}{|z|}\delta(|z|),

(3.35)

where we used the control
\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\leq\varepsilon_{1}(n)
that follwos from .
Then, as in
[
6
]
, we have for all
2a\leq r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq\dfrac{b}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}r\delta^{2}(r)\,dr\leq\log(4)(1+\varepsilon_{1}(n))\varepsilon_{1}(n)\int_{B_{r_{2}}\setminus\overline{B}_{r_{1}}(0)}e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx.

(3.36)

The estimate (
4.12
) from
[
46
]
is

\displaystyle e^{\lambda(z)}|\vec{L}(z)-\vec{L}_{|z|}|\leq e^{\lambda(z)}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\nabla\vec{L}|d\mathscr{H}^{1}\leq Ce^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\delta(|z|),

(3.37)

Therefore, we deduce by (
3.36
) and (
3.37
) that the estimate (
4.13
) from
[
46
]
becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}}}|\vec{L}(z)-\vec{L}_{|z|}|^{2}e^{2\lambda(z)}|dz|^{2}\leq Ce^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\int_{\Omega}e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx.

(3.38)

Now, no change in the strategy from
[
6
]
is needed to obtain the pointwise estimate

\displaystyle e^{\lambda}|\vec{L}(z)|\leq\frac{C}{|z|}e^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2},

(3.39)

which implies the bound (that actually precedes it in the proof)

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{L}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq Ce^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

Now, the rest of the proof is unchanged, and we deduce thanks to the Jacobian systems

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta S&=-\star\nabla\vec{n}\cdot\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\Delta\vec{R}&=(-1)^{n}\star\left(\nabla\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}\right)+\star\nabla\vec{n}\cdot\nabla^{\perp}S\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\end{aligned}\right.

(3.40)

that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\|\nabla^{2}S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq Ce^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)^{2}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C_{1}(n)e^{C_{1}(n)E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)^{2}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.\end{aligned}\right.

Recalling the algebraic identity, we have

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda}\vec{H}=\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{\perp}S\cdot\nabla\vec{\Phi}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla\vec{R}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi},

(3.41)

which implies by the Minkowski inequality that

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq Ce^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)^{2}\left(\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\\
&\left\|S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq Ce^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)^{2}\left(\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),\end{aligned}\right.

(3.42)

while the estimate on
\vec{H}
and
\nabla\vec{H}
follow from (
3.41
). Indeed, we first have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq Ce^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

On the other hand, (
3.41
) shows that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda}\partial_{z}\vec{H}

\displaystyle=-2(\partial_{z}\lambda)e^{\lambda}\vec{H}+\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{\perp}(\partial_{z}S)\cdot e^{-\lambda}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}+\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{\perp}S\cdot e^{-\lambda}\nabla(\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi})-\frac{1}{4}\nabla(\partial_{z}\vec{R})\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\nabla\vec{R}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}e^{-\lambda}\nabla^{\perp}(\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}).

Recall the following identities

\displaystyle\vec{H}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{g}\vec{\Phi}=\frac{1}{2}e^{-\lambda}\Delta\vec{\Phi}=2\,e^{-2\lambda}\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}\vec{\Phi}

\displaystyle\vec{h}_{0}=2\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{z}^{2}\vec{\Phi})=2\,\left(\partial_{z}^{2}\vec{\Phi}-2(\partial_{z}\lambda)\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}\right).

Therefore, we estimate by the identity
|\nabla^{2}\vec{\Phi}|^{2}=2|\partial_{z}^{2}\vec{\Phi}|^{2}+2|\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}\vec{\Phi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\lambda}\nabla^{2}\vec{\Phi}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{{1/2}})}

\displaystyle=\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\left\|e^{-\lambda}\vec{h}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}+2\left\|\nabla\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\left\|\nabla\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}\right)\leq 2E.

Using the
L^{2,1}/L^{2,\infty}
duality, we get

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}\left\|\nabla\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\left\|\nabla^{2}S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}\left\|e^{-\lambda}\nabla^{2}\vec{\Phi}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}\left\|e^{-\lambda}\nabla^{2}\vec{\Phi}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+E)e^{2\Gamma_{1}E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Finally, all estimates follow by estimating

\displaystyle(1+E)\leq e^{E}

\displaystyle\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)^{2}\leq\exp\left(2\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\right)

and taking a large enough constant
C_{1}(n)
.
∎

3.3
Pointwise Estimate in Annular Regions for Jacobian Systems

In this section, we formalise the argument contained in
[
31
, Lemma III.
1
]
and prove a general result that allows us to obtain a Hölder-type estimate for all
0<\beta<1
(it will be important in the asymptotic analysis due to the non-integrable weight that we consider).

First, recall a few technical lemmas from
[
31
]
. Indeed, our approach from the previous section does not work in the case of a non-trivial logarithmic component as in
[
25
]
.

Lemma 3.12

(Lemma F.
1
,
[
31
]
)
.

Let
A\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n}),B\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
. Assume that
u:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(0,1).

(3.43)

Then, there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+C\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|\omega\,\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))},

(3.44)

where
\omega(x)=|x|\log\left(1+\frac{1}{|x|}\right)\sqrt{\log\left(1+\log\left(\frac{1}{|x|}\right)\right)}
.

Proof.

Write
u=\varphi+\psi
, where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\varphi&=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\varphi&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

Since
\psi
is a harmonic function on
B(0,1)
, there exists a sequence
\left\{a_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\psi(z)=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}a_{k}z^{k}\right)

Thanks to the identity (
3.2
), we have for all
0\leq a\leq b\leq 1

\displaystyle\int_{B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla\psi|^{2}dx=\pi\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k|a_{k}|^{2}\left(b^{2k}-a^{2k}\right).

Therefore, taking
b=1
and
a=1/2
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0)}|\nabla\psi|^{2}dx=\pi\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{4^{k}}\right),

while (taking
b=1/2
and
a=1/4
)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0)}|\nabla\psi|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left(\frac{1}{4^{k}}-\frac{1}{16^{k}}\right)=\pi\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{|k|}{4^{k}}|a_{k}|^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{4^{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\,\pi\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|k||a_{k}|^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{4^{k}}\right)=\frac{1}{4}\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0)}|\nabla\psi|^{2}dx.

(3.45)

On the other hand, the weighted Wente inequality of
[
31
, Lemma E.
1
]
shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}\leq C_{W}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|\omega\,\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}(0)\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+\frac{3}{2}C_{W}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|\omega\,\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))},

which concludes the proof of the the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.13

(Lemma F.
2
,
[
31
]
)
.

Let
A\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n}),B\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
. Assume that
u:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(0,1).

(3.46)

Then, there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
such that for all
0<\alpha<1
and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.47)

where
A_{k}=B_{2^{-k}}\setminus\overline{B}_{2^{-(k+1)}}(0)
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Thanks to an immediate scaling argument, we deduce that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k+1})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}+C\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-k}))}\left\|\omega_{k}\,\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-k}(0))}.

(3.48)

where
\omega_{k}(x)=\omega(2^{k}x)
. Therefore, an immediate induction shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+C\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)})}\left\|\omega_{k-1-j}\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)}))}.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we deduce that for all
0\leq\alpha\leq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)}))}\left\|\omega_{k-1-j}\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\alpha)j}}\int_{B(0,2^{k-1-j})}|\nabla A|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha\,j}}\int_{B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)})}f(2^{k-1-j}x)|\nabla B|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
f=\omega^{2}
.
Now, for all
\left\{\varphi_{k,j}\right\}_{k,j\in\mathbb{N}}\in L^{2}(B(0,1))
and for all
0<\alpha<1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha\,j}}\int_{B(0,2^{k-1-j})}|\varphi_{k,j}|^{2}dx=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha\,j}}\sum_{l=k-1-j}^{\infty}\int_{A_{l}}|\varphi_{k,j}|^{2}dx=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{k-1-j\leq l\right\}}\int_{A_{l}}|\varphi_{k,j}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=\max\left\{k-1-l,0\right\}}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}\int_{A_{l}}|\varphi_{k,j}|^{2}dx=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}\int_{A_{l}}|\varphi_{k,j}|^{2}dx+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}\int_{A_{l}}|\varphi_{k,j}|^{2}dx.

(3.49)

Applying this inequality to
\left\{\varphi_{k,j}\right\}_{k,j\in\mathbb{N}}=\left\{\nabla A\right\}_{k,j\in\mathbb{N}}
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\alpha)j}}\int_{B(0,2^{k-1-j})}|\nabla A|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla A|^{2}dx\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\alpha)j}}+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla A|^{2}dx\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\alpha)j}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2(1-\alpha)}}{2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\alpha)l}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla A|^{2}+\frac{2^{2(1-\alpha)}}{2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1}\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla A|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{2^{2(1-\alpha)-1}}\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla A|^{2}.

(3.50)

On the other hand, using that for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in A_{l}

\displaystyle f(2^{k-1-j}x)\leq 2^{2(k-1-j-l)}\log^{2}(1+2^{l-(k-j)+2})\log\left(1+(l-(k-j)+2)\log(2)\right),

we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}\int_{B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)})}f(2^{k-1-j}x)|\nabla B|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}2^{-2(l-(k-j)+1)}\log^{2}(1+2^{l-(k-j)+2})\log\left(1+(l-(k-j)+2)\log(2)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}2^{-2(l-(k-j)+1)}\log^{2}(1+2^{l-(k-j)+2})\log\left(1+(l-(k-j)+2)\log(2)\right).

First, we have by the elementary inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x

\displaystyle\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}2^{-2(l-(k-j)+1)}\log^{2}(1+2^{l-(k-j)+2})\log\left(1+(l-(k-j)+2)\log(2)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\log^{3}(2)\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}(l-(k-j)+2)^{3}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}2^{-2(l-(k-j)+1)}\leq\log^{3}(2)\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha(k-1-l)}}\sum_{i=0}^{l}(i+1)^{3}\frac{1}{2^{2(1+\alpha)i}}

\displaystyle\leq\log^{3}(2)\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha(k-1-l)}}\frac{2^{-4(1+\alpha)}+4\cdot 2^{-2(1+\alpha))}+1}{(1-2^{-2(1+\alpha))})^{4}}\leq\frac{176\log^{3}(2)}{27}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha(k-1-l)}}

where we used

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(i+1)^{3}x^{i}=\frac{x^{2}+4x+1}{(1-x)^{4}}.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}2^{-2(l-(k-j)+1)}\log^{2}(1+2^{l-(k-j)+2})\log\left(1+(l-(k-j)+2)\log(2)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\log^{3}(2)2^{-2(l-k-1)}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(l-(k-j)+2)^{3}\frac{1}{2^{2(1+\alpha)j}}

\displaystyle=\log^{3}(2)2^{-2(l-k+1)}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\left(j^{3}+3(l-k+2)j^{2}+3(l-k+2)^{2}j+(l-k+2)^{3}\right)\frac{1}{2^{2(1+\alpha)j}}

\displaystyle=\log^{3}(2)2^{-2(l-k+1)}

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{(k-1)^{3}\beta^{k+3}-(3k^{3}-6k^{2}+4)\beta^{k+2}+(3k^{3}-3k^{2}-3k+1)\beta^{k+1}-k^{3}\beta^{k}+\beta^{3}+4\beta^{2}+\beta}{(1-\beta)^{4}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+3(l-k+2)\frac{-(k-1)^{2}\beta^{k+2}+(2k^{2}-2k-1)\beta^{k+1}-k^{2}\beta^{k}+\beta^{2}+\beta}{(1-\beta)^{3}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+3(l-k+2)^{2}\frac{(k-1)\beta^{k+1}-k\beta^{k}+\beta}{(1-\beta)^{4}}+(l-k+2)^{3}\frac{1-\beta^{k}}{1-\beta}\right)

where
\beta=2^{-2(1+\alpha)}
. Since the series
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{3}\beta^{k}
converges, we deduce that there exists a universal constant independent of
0<\alpha<1
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}2^{-2(l-(k-j)+1)}\log^{2}(1+2^{l-(k-j)+2})\log\left(1+(l-(k-j)+2)\log(2)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+(l-k+1)^{3}\right)\frac{1}{2^{2(l-k+1)}}.

Then, for all
0<\alpha<1
and for all
l\geq k
, we have

\displaystyle(l-k+1)^{3}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-k-1)}}=\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha(l-k+1)}}\frac{(l-k+1)^{3}}{2^{2(1-\alpha)(l-k-1)}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha(l-k+1)}}\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{(l-k+1)^{3}}{2^{2(1-\alpha)(l-k+1)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha(l-k+1)}}\frac{2^{-4(1-\alpha)}+4\cdot 2^{-2(1-\alpha)}+1}{(1-2^{-2(1-\alpha)})^{4}}=\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha(l-k+1)}}\frac{2^{4(1-\alpha)}+4\cdot 2^{6(1-\alpha)}+2^{8(1-\alpha)}}{(2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1)^{4}}

Therefore, we finally deduce that there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha j}}\int_{B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)})}f(2^{k-1-j}x)|\nabla B|^{2}dx\leq\frac{C}{(2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1)^{4}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}dx.

(3.51)

Thanks to (
3.3
) and (
3.51
), we deduce that for all
0<\alpha<1

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)}))}\left\|\omega_{k-1-j}\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.52)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+\frac{C}{(2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using the elementary inequality

\displaystyle 2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1\geq 2(1-\alpha)\log(2),

the lemma is proved.
∎

Remark 3.14
.

The proof is symmetric in
A
and
B
, and we also get

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+\frac{C}{(2^{2(1-\alpha)}-1)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla A|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Corollary 3.15
.

Let
A\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n}),B\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
. Assume that
u:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(0,1).

(3.53)

Then, there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
such that for all
0<\alpha<1
and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
0\leq j\leq k

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k-j})}+\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-j+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.54)

where
A_{k}=B_{2^{-k}}\setminus\overline{B}_{2^{-(k+1)}}(0)
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Iterating (
3.48
) only
0\leq j\leq k
times, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k-j})}+C\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\frac{1}{2^{i}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-i)})}\left\|\omega_{k}\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)})},

which shows the expected result thanks to (
3.3
).
∎

We can now state the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 3.16
.

Let
0<4\,a<b\leq 1
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
0<t\leq 1
, let
\Omega_{t}=B_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}\,a}(0)
. Let
A\in W^{1,(2,1)}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
B\in W^{1,2}(\Omega,M_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
, and assume that

\displaystyle\Delta A=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A\qquad\text{in}\;\,\Omega.

(3.55)

Let

\displaystyle\Lambda=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla A|}{|x|}dx.

Then, there exists universal constants
C,\Gamma<\infty
such that for all
0<\delta<1
, and for all
0<\beta<1
, if

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma}{(1-\beta)^{7}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\leq\delta^{2},

(3.56)

then for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{|z|}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{\left(1+C\delta\right)}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\,\Lambda+C\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right).

(3.57)

Remark 3.17
.

The hypothesis
b\leq 1
is not restrictive for the previous lemmas apply provided that the conformal class of the annulus is bounded from below.

Proof.

Make on
\Omega
a global expansion

\displaystyle\nabla A=\nabla\varphi+\nabla\psi,

where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\varphi&=\nabla^{\perp}\widetilde{B}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{A}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\varphi&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

(3.58)

and
\widetilde{A}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\widetilde{B}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow M_{n}(\mathbb{R})
are controlled extensions of
A
and
B
respectively given by Lemma
3.6
. There exists a universal constant
C<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\leq C\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.59)

Now, if

\displaystyle d=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,r)}\partial_{\nu}\psi\,d\mathscr{H}^{1},

and
\psi_{0}=\psi-d\,\log|z|
,
then thanks to the proof of Corollary
3.3
, we deduce that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}

\displaystyle|z||\nabla\psi_{0}(z)|\leq\frac{8}{3\sqrt{3\pi}}\left(\frac{|z|}{b}+\frac{a}{|z|}\right)\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

which implies that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
A_{k}=B_{2^{-k}}\setminus\overline{B}_{2^{-(k+1)}}(0)\subset\Omega_{1/2}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq 3\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.60)

Notice also that thanks to Parseval identity, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+C\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Then, thanks to Lemma
3.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq 64\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{15}}\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq 64\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{15}}\left(1+C\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.61)

Therefore, thanks to the
L^{2,1}/L^{2,\infty}
duality and (
3.59
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|}{|x|}dx\leq\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\left\|\frac{1}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq\sqrt{\pi}C\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(3.62)

while (
3.61
) shows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\psi_{0}|}{|x|}dx\leq 64\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{15}}\left(1+C\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.63)

Therefore, we have by (
3.62
) and (
3.63
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla u-\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})|}{|x|}dx\leq\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|}{|x|}dx+\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\psi_{0}|}{|x|}dx\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(3.64)

which implies in particular thanks to the triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\left|2\pi\,d\,\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)-2\pi\Lambda\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|}dx-\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})|}{|x|}dx\right|\leq\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla u-\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})|}{|x|}dx

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.65)

Now,
we deduce thanks to Lemma
3.13
that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
0<\alpha<1
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla\widetilde{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.66)

Since
\mathrm{supp}(\nabla\widetilde{A})\subset B_{2b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a/2}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx=0

provided that
2^{-l}\leq a/2
, or
l\geq\log_{2}\left(\dfrac{2}{a}\right)
. Likewise,

\displaystyle\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx=0

provided that
2^{-l-1}\geq 2b
, or
l\leq\log_{2}\left(\dfrac{1}{4b}\right)
. If
N_{2}=\left[\log_{2}\left(\dfrac{2}{a}\right)\right]
, and
N_{1}=\left[\log_{2}\left(\dfrac{1}{4b}\right)\right]
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx=\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx.

Then, provided that
A_{k}\subset\Omega_{1/2}
, we have by (
3.61
), (
3.60
), and (
3.66
) the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}+\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}+\left\|\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+3\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)\left(1+C\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla\widetilde{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(3.67)

where we used
0<b<1
, to bound the first term, and thanks to (
3.3
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2\pi\,d^{2}\int_{2^{-(k+1)}}^{2^{-k}}\frac{d\rho}{\rho}}=\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\,|d|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

Now, let us prove a variant of
[
31
, Lemma G.
1
]
.

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
0<s<t<1
,
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}
, and
\left\{a_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}},\left\{b_{k}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}
be two sequences. Assume that the following inequality holds for all
N_{1}\leq k\leq N_{2}

\displaystyle a_{k}\leq b_{k}+\Gamma\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}s^{|n-k+1|}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.68)

where
\Gamma<\infty
is a fixed real number independent of
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, we have for all
N_{1}\leq k\leq N_{2}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}t^{|l-k+1|}a_{l}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}t^{|l-k+1|}b_{l}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\,\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{1-s\,t}+\frac{1}{1-s\,t^{-1}}\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}t^{|l-k+1|}a_{l}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.69)

Proof.

Thanks to Minkowski’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}t^{|l-k+1|}a_{l}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}t^{|l-k+1|}b_{k}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Gamma\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}t^{|l-k+1|}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}s^{|n-k+1|}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}t^{|l-k+1|}b_{k}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Gamma\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}^{2}\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}s^{|n-k+1|}t^{|l-k+1|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and thanks to the proof of
[
31
, Lemma G.
1
]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}s^{|n-k+1|}t^{|l-k+1|}\leq\left(\frac{2}{1-s\,t}+\frac{1}{1-s\,t^{-1}}\right)\,t^{|n-k+1|},

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Since
\widetilde{A}=A
on
\Omega
, if

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &s=\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha}}\\
&a_{k}=\left\|\nabla\widetilde{A}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\\
&b_{k}=C\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\\
&\Gamma=\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\end{aligned}\right.

then the estimate (
3.68
) holds provided that
A_{k}\subset\Omega_{1/2}
, or

\displaystyle 2^{-k}\leq\frac{b}{2}\qquad\text{and}\;\,2^{-k-1}\geq 2a,

which gives

\displaystyle\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{b}\right)\leq k\leq\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{4a}\right)

Recalling that
N_{1}=\left[\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{4b}\right)\right]
and
N_{2}=\left[\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{a}\right)\right]
, we deduce that
A_{k}\subset\Omega_{1/2}
if and only if
N_{1}+3\leq k\leq N_{2}-3
. Therefore, we deduce thanks to the lemma that for all
0<\beta<\alpha<1
, there holds thanks to Minkowski’s inequality

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}+3}^{N_{2}-3}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\left(\frac{a}{2^{-l}}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta}+\frac{1}{\alpha-\beta}\right)\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore, if we choose
\alpha=\beta+\frac{1-\beta}{2}=\frac{1+\beta}{2}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{C}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta}+\frac{1}{\alpha-\beta}\right)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{(1-\beta)^{\frac{7}{2}}},

so for all
0<\delta<1
, assuming that

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime}}{(1-\beta)^{\frac{7}{2}}}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{1+\delta}\right),

(3.70)

we deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}+3}^{N_{2}-3}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(1+\delta)\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\times\left(\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\left(\frac{a}{2^{-l}}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+(1+\delta)\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle+\delta\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{1}+2}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx+\sum_{l=N_{2}-2}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.71)

From this inequality which corresponds to
[
31
, Proposition III.
1
]
, the rest of the proof is unchanged. However, for the sake of completeness, let us give the remaining details.
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}=\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1-l)}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}+\sum_{l=k}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(l+1-k)}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}.

Recalling that
N_{1}=\left[\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{4b}\right)\right]
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1-l)}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1)}}\frac{1}{b^{2}}\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\beta)l}}=\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1)}}\frac{1}{b^{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\beta)N_{1}}}\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{k--N_{1}-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\beta)l^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1)}}\frac{1}{b^{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\beta)N_{1}}}\frac{2^{2(1-\beta)}}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}=\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1)}}\frac{1}{b^{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2(1-\beta)}\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{4b}\right)}\frac{2^{2(1-\beta)}}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1)}}\frac{1}{b^{2}}(4b)^{2(1-\beta)}\frac{2^{2(1-\beta)}}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}=\frac{2^{2(3-2\beta)}}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{2\beta}.

(3.72)

Then, trivially have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=k}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(l+1-k)}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{2}\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2(1+\beta)l^{\prime}}}=\frac{2^{2(1+\beta)}}{2^{2(1+\beta)}-1}\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{2^{2(1+\beta)}}{2^{2(1+\beta)}-1}\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{2\beta}.

(3.73)

since
0<\beta<1
. Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1-l)}}\left(\frac{a}{2^{-l}}\right)^{2}=a^{2}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(k-1)}}2^{2\beta N_{1}}\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{k-N_{1}-1}2^{2(1+\beta)l^{\prime}}=a^{2}\frac{2^{2\beta}}{2^{2\beta(k-N_{1})}}\frac{2^{(1+\beta)(k-N_{1})}-1}{2^{2(1+\beta)}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2\beta}}{2^{2(1+\beta)}-1}\left(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{4}{3}\left(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)^{2\beta}.

(3.74)

Finally, since
N_{2}=\left[\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{a}\right)\right]
, we have the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{l=k}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(l-k+1)}}\left(\frac{a}{2^{-l}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=a^{2}2^{2\beta(k-1)}\sum_{l=k}^{N_{2}}2^{2(1-\beta)l}=a^{2}2^{2\beta(k-1)}2^{2(1-\beta)k}\frac{2^{2(1-\beta)(N_{2}-k+1)}-1}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2(1-2\beta)}}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}a^{2}2^{2\beta k}2^{2(1-\beta)N_{2}}=\frac{2^{2(1-2\beta)}}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}a^{2}2^{2\beta k}\left(\frac{2}{a}\right)^{2(1-\beta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2(2-3\beta)}}{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}\left(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)^{2\beta}.

(3.75)

Therefore, thanks to (
3.3
), (
3.73
), (
3.3
), (
3.3
), and the elementary inequality
\sqrt{x+y}\leq\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\left(\frac{2^{-l}}{b}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-l}}\bigg)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2^{\beta}}{\sqrt{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}}+\frac{2^{3-2\beta}}{\sqrt{2^{2(1-\beta)}-1}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(2+4\sqrt{\frac{2}{\log(2)}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)^{\beta}\right),

(3.76)

where we used the elementary inequality
2^{2(1-\beta)}\geq 1+2\log(2)(1-\beta)
. Then, we trivially have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2^{2\beta}}{2^{2\beta}-1}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\log(2)}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta}}.

Then, we can estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{k=N_{1}}^{N_{1}+2}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\leq\frac{3}{2^{2\beta(k-1)}}2^{2\beta N_{1}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla A|^{2}dx=3\cdot 2^{2\beta}\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{2\beta}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla A|^{2}dx,

(3.77)

where we used the extra property from the extension Lemma
3.6
. Finally, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=N_{2}-2}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k-1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\leq 3\cdot 2^{2\beta(k-1)}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(N_{2}-2)}}\int_{B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0)}|\nabla A|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=3\cdot 2^{2\beta(k-1)}2^{4\beta}\left(\frac{a}{2}\right)^{2\beta}\int_{B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0)}|\nabla A|^{2}dx=3\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)^{2\beta}\int_{B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0)}|\nabla A|^{2}dx

(3.78)

Gathering (
3.3
), (
3.3
), (
3.77
), and (
3.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(\sum_{l=N_{1}}^{N_{1}+2}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx+\sum_{l=N_{2}-2}^{N_{2}}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\delta)\left(2+4\sqrt{\frac{2}{\log(2)}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)^{\beta}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+(1+\delta)\sqrt{\frac{2^{2\beta}}{2^{2\beta}-1}}\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle+(1+\delta)\sqrt{3}(1+2^{\beta})\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)^{\beta}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0))}+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\right).

(3.79)

Now, coming back to (
3.3
), and applying the inequality for
\alpha=\beta
(this step explains why we had to precisely keep track of constants), we finally obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq C\left\{\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)+\frac{1}{(1-\beta)^{3}}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)^{\beta}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\times\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left(1+\frac{C}{(1-\beta)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\sqrt{\frac{2^{2\beta}}{2^{2\beta}-1}}\right)\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)+\sqrt{1-\beta}\left(1-\frac{1}{1+\delta}\right)\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)\right)\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+\left(1+C\sqrt{1-\beta}\left(1-\frac{1}{1+\delta}\right)\right)\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

where we used (
3.70
).
Finally, using the elementary inequality

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{2}\leq 1-\frac{1}{1+\delta}\leq\delta\quad\text{for all}\;\,0<\delta<1,

we deduce by (
3.70
) that there exists universal constants
C,\Gamma<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\Gamma\,\delta(1-\beta)^{\frac{7}{2}}

implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)+\delta\sqrt{1-\beta}\left(\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\bigg(\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\bigg)^{\beta}\right)\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+\left(1+C\delta\sqrt{1-\beta}\right)\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

that we can also rewrite (for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}
) as

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{|z|}(0))}\leq C\left(\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)+\frac{a}{|z|}\right)+\delta\sqrt{1-\beta}\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+\left(1+C\delta\sqrt{1-\beta}\right)\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\,\Lambda+C\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

(3.80)

which finally concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

(
1
)

For
B=0
, we recover the estimate for harmonic functions from Corollary (
3.4
). Indeed, if

\displaystyle u(z)=d\,\log|z|+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}z^{k}\right)=d\,\log|z|+u_{0}(z),

we can directly estimate thanks to Lemma
3.5

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla u_{0}|}{|x|}dx\leq C\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

while

\displaystyle|d|\,\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla(u-u_{0})|}{|x|}dx,

which implies the general bound that includes the logarithm component.

(
2
)

Another reason why we were careful in the proof is to obtain an optimal constant for the upper semi-continuity result in the case of collars. Let us take the example of harmonic maps into
S^{n}
to make the result clearer. If
u:\Sigma\rightarrow S^{n-1}
is a harmonic map, thanks to the conservation law

\displaystyle\dive(u_{i}\nabla u_{j}-u_{j}\nabla u_{i})=0\quad\text{for all}\;\,1\leq i,j\leq n

we can (locally) rewrite the equation
-\Delta u=|\nabla u|^{2}u
as

\displaystyle-\Delta u=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla u,

where
\mathrm{curl}B_{i,j}=u_{i}\nabla u_{j}-u_{j}\nabla u_{i}
for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
. Now, let us define the optimal constant in the
\varepsilon
-regularity on annuli. We have the following result (
[
20
]
) that follows from the standard one on balls.

Theorem 3.20
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and
u:\Omega\rightarrow S^{n-1}
be a harmonic map. There exists
\varepsilon_{0}(n)>0
and a universal constant
C(n)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0}(n)

(3.81)

implies that for all
2a<|z|<b/2

\displaystyle|z||\nabla u(z)|\leq C(n)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{3|z|/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{|z|/2}(0))}.

(3.82)

Recall that the second derivative of the Dirichlet energy for harmonic maps into spheres is given by

\displaystyle Q_{u}(w)=\int_{\Sigma}\left(|dw|^{2}_{g}-|du|_{g}^{2}|w|^{2}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Therefore, if
C(n)<\infty
is the optimal constant in this inequality, the proof of
[
31
, Lemma V.
1
]
and
[
31
, Lemma IV.
1
]
(see Theorem
1.1
above) shows that
\Lambda^{\ast}
can be estimated from below by

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\ast}\geq\Lambda,

where
\Lambda
is such that

\displaystyle\Lambda\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}C(n)\leq\pi,

which gives us the estimate

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\ast}\geq\frac{1}{C(n)}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2\log(2)}}.

In other words, if
\mathcal{L}_{k}
is the maximum length of the
images
of collars by a sequence of harmonic maps
\left\{u_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
from
\Sigma
into
S^{n-1}
is such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{L}_{k}<\frac{1}{C(n)}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2\log(2)}},

then
[
31
, Theorem I.
2
]
holds true (assuming that the other hypotheses of the theorem hold).
We believe that this estimate is optimal. Due to the instability of geodesics of length larger than
\pi
on the sphere
S^{2}
, we expect that

\displaystyle C(2)=\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}=2.08690\cdots

Notice that this estimate is verified in all cases covered in
[
31
]
(although it will be slightly modified depending on the shape of the second derivative) and works for Willmore immersions too.

3.4
Extension of the Results for General Divergence Equations

In this section, we extend the previous results to an equation of the form

\displaystyle\Delta u=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A+\dive(C),

where
C\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),M_{n,2}(\mathbb{R}))
, and
\dive C=(\dive C_{1},\cdots,\dive C_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
C_{i}\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{2})
. In the case of collar regions, the non-trivial residue makes such a divergence quantity in the weak Willmore equation (
[
25
]
).

Let us start by proving a variant of
[
31
, Lemma E.
1
]
for another type of divergence equations.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
C\in L^{2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{2})
, and let
u:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a solution of the equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta u&=\dive(C)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

(3.83)

Then, for all
\alpha>2\sqrt{2}=2.828\cdots
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\leq 25\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}+1}{\alpha^{2}-8}\right)^{2}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|C|^{2}dx.

Proof.

First, by
[
47
, Lemma
5.6
]
, for all
\beta>-2
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx=-\frac{2}{\beta+2}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u\,(x\cdot\nabla u)dx,

(3.84)

which also implies by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx\leq\frac{4}{(\beta+2)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\left(x\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx\leq\frac{4}{(\beta+2)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx.

(3.85)

Now, integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}u\,\Delta u\,dx-(\beta+2)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u(x\cdot\nabla u)dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}u\,\Delta u\,dx+2\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx.

(3.86)

Using the Euler-Lagrange equation and integrating by parts again, we deduce that

\displaystyle-\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}u\,\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}u\,\dive(C)\,dx

\displaystyle=(\beta+2)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u(x\cdot C)dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}\nabla u\cdot C\,dx.

(3.87)

Therefore, by (
3.4
), (
3.4
), and Cauchy’s inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx=(\beta+2)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u(x\cdot C)\,dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}\nabla u\cdot C+2\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx+\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|C|^{2}dx+(\beta+2)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u(x\cdot C)\,dx+2\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx,

which shows by another application of Cauchy’s inequality that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|C|^{2}dx+\frac{\beta+2}{1-\varepsilon}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u(x\cdot C)\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+(\beta+2)^{2}}{4\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|C|^{2}dx+\frac{2+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx.

(3.88)

Putting together (
3.85
) and (
3.4
), we finally deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx\leq\frac{1+(\beta+2)^{2}}{\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)(\beta+2)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|C|^{2}dx+\frac{2+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{4}{(\beta+2)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx.

(3.89)

Since

\displaystyle\frac{8}{(\beta+2)^{2}}<1

if and only if
\beta>2(\sqrt{2}-1)
, and
0<\varepsilon<1
is arbitrary, we deduce that for all
\beta>2(\sqrt{2}-1)
, there exists
0<\varepsilon_{\beta}<1
such that

\displaystyle\frac{2+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{4}{(\beta+2)^{2}}<1.

Explicitly, we can choose

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\beta}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\beta+2)^{2}-8}{4+(\beta+2)^{2}},

which yields by (
3.89
) the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}u^{2}dx\leq\frac{1+(\beta+2)^{2}}{\varepsilon_{\beta}(1-\varepsilon_{\beta})(\beta+2)^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2+\varepsilon_{\beta}}{1-\varepsilon_{\beta}}\frac{4}{(\beta+2)^{2}}\right)^{-1}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta+2}|C|^{2}dx.

(3.90)

Make the change of variable
\alpha=\beta+2
. Let us estimate the constant explicitly. We have

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\alpha^{2}-8}{\alpha^{2}+4},

which implies that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2}\frac{2(\alpha^{2}+4)-(\alpha^{2}-8)}{\alpha^{2}+4}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\alpha^{2}+16}{\alpha^{2}+4}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}=\frac{2(\alpha^{2}+4)}{\alpha^{2}+16},

while

\displaystyle 2+\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2}\frac{4(\alpha^{2}+4)+\alpha^{2}-8}{\alpha^{2}+4}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{5\alpha^{2}+8}{\alpha^{2}+4}

so that

\displaystyle\frac{2+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}=\frac{5\alpha^{2}+8}{\alpha^{2}+16}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}=\frac{4(5\alpha^{2}+8)}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)},

and finally

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}=\frac{\alpha^{4}-4\alpha^{2}-32}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)}=\frac{(\alpha^{2}-8)(\alpha^{2}+4)}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)}

Then, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)=\frac{1}{4}\frac{(\alpha^{2}+16)(\alpha^{2}-8)}{(\alpha^{2}+4)^{2}}

\displaystyle\frac{1+\alpha^{2}}{\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)\alpha^{2}}=\frac{4(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)^{2}}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)(\alpha^{2}-8)},

and finally

\displaystyle\frac{1+\alpha^{2}}{\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)\alpha^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)^{2}}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)(\alpha^{2}-8)}\times\frac{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)}{(\alpha^{2}+4)(\alpha^{2}-8)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)}{(\alpha^{2}-8)^{2}},

which shows that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha-2}u^{2}dx\leq\frac{4(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)}{(\alpha^{2}-8)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|C|^{2}dx,

(3.91)

and finally, (
3.4
) and (
3.91
) show that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)^{2}}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)(\alpha^{2}-8)}+\frac{5\alpha^{2}+8}{\alpha^{2}+16}\times\frac{4(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)}{(\alpha^{2}-8)^{2}}\right)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|C|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)(\alpha^{2}-8)^{2}}\left((\alpha^{2}+4)(\alpha^{2}-8)+4\alpha^{2}(5\alpha^{2}+8)\right)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|C|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)(\alpha^{2}-8)^{2}}\left(21\alpha^{4}+28\alpha^{2}-32\right)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|C|^{2}dx.

(3.92)

Therefore, (
3.90
) and (
3.4
) show that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\leq\frac{(\alpha^{2}+1)(\alpha^{2}+4)}{\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16)(\alpha^{2}-8)^{2}}\left(25\alpha^{4}+102\alpha^{2}-32\right)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|C|^{2}dx.

Since

\displaystyle(\alpha^{2}+4)(25\alpha^{4}+102\alpha^{2})=25\alpha^{6}+202\alpha^{4}+376\alpha^{2}\leq 25\alpha^{6}+425\alpha^{4}+400\alpha^{2}=25(\alpha^{2}+1)\alpha^{2}(\alpha^{2}+16),

the proof is complete.
∎

Remark 3.22
.

The same proof combined with the
L^{\infty}
Wente estimate shows that for all
a,b\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1))
, if
u
solves

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta u&=\nabla^{\perp}a\cdot\nabla b\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1),\end{aligned}\right.

(3.93)

then for all
\alpha>2\sqrt{2}
and for all
0\leq\beta\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\alpha}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\leq C_{\alpha}\left\|\nabla a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla b\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{2\alpha\beta}|\nabla a|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{2\alpha(1-\beta)}|\nabla b|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

We can now extend Lemma
3.12
.

Lemma 3.23
.

Let
A\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
B\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
, and
C\in L^{2}(B(0,1),M_{n,2}(\mathbb{R}))
and assume that
u:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A+\dive(C)\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(0,1).

(3.94)

Then, there exists a universal constant
\Gamma<\infty
such that for all
\beta>\sqrt{2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+\Gamma\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|\omega\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle+2^{2\beta+4}\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-2}\left\||x|^{\beta}C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))},

(3.95)

where
\omega(x)=|x|\log\left(1+\frac{1}{|x|}\right)\sqrt{\log\left(1+\log\left(\frac{1}{|x|}\right)\right)}
.

Proof.

Make a decomposition
u=\varphi+\psi+\chi
, where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\varphi&=\nabla^{\perp}A\cdot\nabla B\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1),\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\chi&=\dive(C)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

Since
\psi
is a harmonic function on the ball
B(0,1)
, the inequality (
3.3
) shows that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0)}|\nabla\psi|^{2}dx\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0)}|\nabla\psi|^{2}dx.

(3.96)

On the other hand, we have by
[
31
, Lemma E.
1
]

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}dx\leq C\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla A|^{2}dx\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla B|^{2}\omega^{2}dx,

(3.97)

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0)}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}dx\leq 16\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0)}|x|^{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}dx\leq 16C\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla A|^{2}dx\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla B|^{2}\omega^{2}dx.

(3.98)

Likewise, Lemma
3.21
implies that for all
\beta>2\sqrt{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0)}|\nabla\chi|^{2}\leq 2^{2\beta}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}|\nabla\chi|^{2}dx\leq 2^{2\beta+5}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-8}\right)^{2}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}|C|^{2}dx.

(3.99)

Gathering (
3.96
), (
3.98
), and (
3.99
), we deduce by Minkowski’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}+\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+\frac{3}{2}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}+\frac{3}{2}\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1/2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/4}(0))}
