\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{1/2}(0))}+6\sqrt{C}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|\omega\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}2^{\beta+\frac{5}{2}}\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-8}\left\||x|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

Replacing
\beta
by
2\beta
, the last constant becomes

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}2^{2\beta+\frac{5}{2}}\frac{4\beta^{2}+1}{4\beta^{2}-8}\leq 2^{2\beta+4}\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-2},

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Let us now extend Lemma
3.13
.

Lemma 3.24
.

Let
A\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
B\in W^{1,2}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
, and
C\in L^{2}(B(0,1),M_{n,2}(\mathbb{R}))
and assume that
u:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A+\dive(C)\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(0,1).

(3.100)

Then, there exists a universal constant
\Gamma<\infty
such that for all
0<\alpha<1
and for all
\beta>\sqrt{2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+\frac{\Gamma}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Gamma\frac{2^{2\beta}}{\sqrt{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-2}\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(3.101)

where
A_{k}=B_{2^{-k}}\setminus\overline{B}_{2^{-(k+1)}}(0)
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Fix some
\beta>\sqrt{2}
, and apply Lemma
3.23
and an immediate scaling argument to deduce that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}+\Gamma\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-k}))}\left\|\omega_{k}\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-k}))}

\displaystyle+2^{2\beta+4}\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-2}\left\||2^{k}x|^{\beta}C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-k}))},

where
\omega_{k}(x)=\omega(2^{k}x)
. Therefore, an immediate induction shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}+C\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)}))}\left\|\omega_{k-1-j}\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)}))}

\displaystyle+2^{2\beta+4}\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\||2^{k-1-j}x|^{\beta}C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)}))}.

(3.102)

We estimate as in Lemma
3.13
the second component of the right-hand side of (
3.4
). Now, using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we deduce that for all
0<\delta<1
, there holds

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\left\||2^{k-1-j}x|^{\beta}C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,2^{-(k-1-j)}))}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{1}{2^{(1-\delta)j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{B(0,2^{k-1-j})}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2^{1-\delta}}{2^{1-\delta}-1}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{B(0,2^{k-1-j})}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.103)

Now, using the identity (
3.3
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{B(0,2^{k-1-j})}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{A_{l}}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{A_{l}}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx.

(3.104)

We first estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{A_{l}}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}2^{2\beta(k-1-j)}.

(3.105)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)j}}=\frac{1}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)(k-1-l)}}\sum_{i=0}^{l}\frac{1}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)i}}\leq\frac{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)(k-1-l)}},

(3.106)

so that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\int_{A_{l}}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx\sum_{j=k-1-l}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}2^{2\beta(k-1-j)}\leq\frac{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}-1}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta(k-1-l)}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx.

(3.107)

On the other hand, there holds

\displaystyle\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{A_{l}}|2^{k-1-j}x|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx\leq\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(l-k+1)}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)j}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}-1}\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(l-k+1)}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx.

(3.108)

Finally, we have by (
3.4
), (
3.4
), (
3.105
), (
3.107
), and (
3.4
)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta j}}\int_{B(0,2^{k-1-j})}|2^{k-1-j}|^{2\beta}|C|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}}{2^{2(\beta+\delta)}-1}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\frac{1}{2^{2\delta(l-k+1)}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta(l-k+1)}}\int_{A_{l}}|C|^{2}dx\right).

(3.109)

Since
\beta>\sqrt{2}
, the theorem follows by taking
\alpha=\delta
.
∎

Finally, we can prove a generalisation of Theorem
3.16
.

Theorem 3.25
.

Let
0<2\,a<b\leq 1
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
0<t\leq 1
, let
\Omega_{t}=B_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}\,a}(0)
. Let
A\in W^{1,(2,1)}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
B\in W^{1,2}(\Omega,M_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
,
C\in L^{2,1}(\Omega,M_{n,2}(\mathbb{R}))
and assume that

\displaystyle\Delta A=\nabla^{\perp}B\cdot\nabla A+\dive(C)\qquad\text{in}\;\,\Omega.

Let

\displaystyle\Lambda=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla A|}{|x|}dx.

Then, there exists a universal constants
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}<\infty
such that for all
0<\delta<1
, and for all
0<\beta<1
, if

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{0}}{(1-\beta)^{7}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla B|^{2}dx\leq\delta^{2},

(3.110)

then there exists
C_{\beta}<\infty
such that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{|z|}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\left(1+C\delta\right)}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\,\Lambda+\Gamma_{1}\left(\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\right)\right).

(3.111)

Proof.

On
\Omega
, make a global expansion

\displaystyle A=\nabla\varphi+\nabla\chi+\nabla\psi,

where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\varphi&=\nabla^{\perp}\widetilde{B}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{A}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\varphi&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1),\end{aligned}\right.

(3.112)

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\chi&=\dive(C)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\chi&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\end{aligned}\right.

(3.113)

where
\widetilde{A}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\widetilde{B}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow M_{n}(\mathbb{R})
are controlled extensions of
A
and
B
given by Lemma
3.6
, while
\widetilde{C}
is the extension of
C
by
0
. We have as in (
3.59
)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\leq\Gamma_{0}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(3.114)

while Calderón-Zygmund estimates and interpolation theory show that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\leq\Gamma_{0}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1)))}\leq\Gamma_{0}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}.

(3.115)

Then, if for some
a\leq r\leq b

\displaystyle d=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,r)}\partial_{\nu}\psi\,d\mathscr{H}^{1},

and
\psi_{0}=\psi-d_{0}\log|z|
, we obtain as in (
3.60
) that all
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
A_{k}=B_{2^{-k}}\setminus\overline{B}_{2^{-(k+1)}}(0)\subset\Omega_{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq 3\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(3.116)

while Parseval identity implies that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\Gamma_{0}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\Gamma_{0}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

As previously, using Lemma
3.1
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq 64\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{15}}\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq 64\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{15}}\left(1+\Gamma_{0}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\Gamma_{0}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.117)

Now, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\chi|}{|x|}dx\leq\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\left\|\frac{1}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma_{0}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}.

(3.118)

As previously (see (
3.62
) and (
3.63
)), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|}{|x|}dx\leq\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma_{0}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(3.119)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\psi_{0}|}{|x|}dx\leq 64\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{15}}\left(1+\Gamma_{0}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+64\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{15}}\Gamma_{0}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}.

(3.120)

Finally, we deduce by (
3.118
), (
3.119
), and (
3.120
), we have by the triangle inequality

\displaystyle\left|2\pi d\,\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)-2\pi\,\Lambda\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|}dx-\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})|}{|x|}dx\right|\leq\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla u-\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})|}{|x|}dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|}{|x|}dx+\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\chi|}{|x|}dx+\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla\psi_{0}|}{|x|}dx

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{1}\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\Gamma_{1}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}.

(3.121)

Now, using Lemma
3.24
, we deduce that for all
0<\alpha<1
and
\beta>\sqrt{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\varphi+\chi)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left\|\nabla(\varphi+\chi)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{0})}+\frac{\Gamma}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla\widetilde{B}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Gamma\frac{2^{2\beta}}{\sqrt{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-1}\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\widetilde{C}|^{2}dx+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\widetilde{C}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.122)

Proceeding as in the proof of Theorem
3.16
, we finally deduce that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
A_{k}\subset\Omega_{1/2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}\leq\left\|\nabla(\varphi+\chi)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}+\left\|\nabla\psi_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}+\left\|\nabla(\psi-\psi_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\left(\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}+\left\|\nabla\chi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\right)+3\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)\left((1+\Gamma_{0}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)})\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\Gamma_{0}\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\Gamma}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Gamma\frac{2^{2\beta}}{\sqrt{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-1}\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\widetilde{C}|^{2}dx+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\widetilde{C}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\left(\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{2}\left(\frac{2^{-k}}{b}+\frac{a}{2^{-k}}\right)\left(\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\Gamma}{(1-\alpha)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\nabla\widetilde{A}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Gamma\frac{2^{2\beta}}{\sqrt{1-\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta^{2}+1}{\beta^{2}-1}\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta l}}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\widetilde{C}|^{2}dx+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{2\beta|l-k+1|}}\int_{A_{l}}|\widetilde{C}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2\pi\log(2)}\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}+\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\left(\left(1+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|C\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\right).

From now on, all steps of the proof of Theorem
3.16
are identical and we skip the details.
∎

3.5
Pointwise Estimate of the Mean Curvature:Neck Regions Case

In this section, we refine the work of
[
46
]
that gives a pointwise estimate of the metric in the neck regions thanks to the previous general theorem. Recall that in a neck region
\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}(0)
, there exists an integer
m\geq 1
and
\alpha_{0}>0
such that for
0<\alpha<\alpha_{0}
and all
k
large enough,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\lambda_{k}-(m-1)\log|z|)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}\leq C\left(\sqrt{\alpha}\left\|\nabla\lambda_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}+\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}|^{2}dx\right).

We will also need the improved energy quantization

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}=0.

(3.123)

Explicitly, we prove in
[
46
, Theorem
4.1
]
that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}\leq Ce^{C\,E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(4\alpha))}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(4\alpha))},

where

\displaystyle E=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\left\|\nabla\lambda_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{k}(1))}+\int_{\Omega_{k}(1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}|^{2}dx\right)<\infty,

which allows us to deduce the
L^{2,1}
quantization from the
L^{2}
quantization first proved in
[
6
]
.

Theorem 3.26
.

Let
0<2\,a<b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
0<t\leq 1
, defined
\Omega_{t}=\Omega_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}a}
. Let
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,b)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a weak conformal Willmore immersion. Define

\displaystyle E=\left\|\nabla\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}dx.

For all
0<\beta<1
, there exists
\varepsilon_{1}(\beta,n)>0
and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C_{k}(E,n)
such that the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\varepsilon_{1}(\beta,n)

(3.124)

implies that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}
,

\displaystyle e^{\lambda(z)}|\nabla^{k}\vec{H}(z)|\leq\frac{C_{k}(E,n)}{|z|^{k}}\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.125)

Proof.

Recall that
A=(\vec{R},S):B(0,1)\rightarrow(\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R}=\mathbb{R}^{N(n)}
solves the system of equations

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta S&=-\star\nabla\vec{n}\cdot\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\Delta\vec{R}&=(-1)^{n}\star\left(\nabla\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}\right)+\star\nabla\vec{n}\cdot\nabla^{\perp}S\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\end{aligned}\right.

(3.126)

Therefore,
A
solves a system of the form (
3.55
) in Theorem
3.16
(where the coefficients of
B
are linear combinations of coefficients of
\vec{n}
). Furthermore, the estimate (
3.56
) is equivalent to (
3.124
). Therefore, we can apply Theorem
3.16
to
A=(\vec{R},S)
which gives us the estimate for all
z\in\Omega_{1/4}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\vec{R},S)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}\right)\left\|\nabla(\vec{R},S)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(\Lambda+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{C}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\left(\Lambda+\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

(3.127)

where

\displaystyle\Lambda=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{|\nabla A|}{|x|}dx,

(3.128)

where we used the estimate (
3.26
) from Theorem
3.10
. Thanks to the
L^{2,1}
bound (
3.27
) from Theorem
3.10
, we deduce thanks to the
L^{2,1}/L^{2,\infty}
duality that

\displaystyle\Lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\left\|\nabla A\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\left\|\frac{1}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}}\left(\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(E)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.129)

Since

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\vec{R},S)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}=\int_{A(2,z)}\left(|\nabla\vec{R}|^{2}+|\nabla S|^{2}\right)dx,

and using the algebraic identity

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda}\vec{H}=\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{\perp}S\cdot\nabla\vec{\Phi}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla\vec{R}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi},

(3.130)

we deduce by (
3.26
) and (
3.5
) that for all
z\in\Omega_{\frac{1}{4}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\leq C_{\beta}(E)\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

(3.131)

Finally, thanks to the precised
\varepsilon
-regularity from Theorem
3.8
, we
have for all
z\in\Omega_{1/4}

\displaystyle e^{\lambda(z)}|\vec{H}(z)|\leq\frac{C_{1}}{|z|}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\leq\frac{C_{\beta}(E)}{|z|}\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

(3.132)

which concludes the proof of the theorem for
k=1
. The case
k>1
is immediate thanks to Theorem
3.8
.
∎

Remark 3.27
.

The theorem was stated on
\Omega_{1/2}
and proved on
\Omega_{1/4}
, but it clearly holds on
\Omega_{\frac{1}{2}}
provided that one uses (
3.26
) and (
3.27
) with
t=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
and chooses worst constants in Theorem
3.16
.

Corollary 3.28
.

Under the hypotheses of Theorem
A
, for all
0<\beta<1
, there exists a universal constant there exists a universal constant
\alpha_{0}=\alpha_{0}(\beta)>0
, and
C_{\beta}<\infty
such that for all
0<\alpha<\alpha_{0}
and for all
z\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)

\displaystyle e^{\lambda_{k}(z)}|\vec{H}_{k}(z)|\leq\frac{C_{\beta}}{|z|^{k}}\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda_{k}}\vec{H}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}.

(3.133)

3.6
Estimate of the Full Second Fundamental Form

3.6.1
The Codimension One Case

In this case, the unit normal takes values into
S^{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Recall that the following equation holds on
B(0,b)

\displaystyle\nabla\vec{n}=\vec{n}\times\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}-2\,H\nabla\vec{\Phi}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\vec{n}=\nabla\vec{n}\times\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}-2\,\dive\left(H\nabla\vec{\Phi}\right).

We are exactly in the setting of Theorem
3.25
, which implies the following statement.

Theorem 3.29
.

Let
0<4\,a<b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
0<t\leq 1
, defined
\Omega_{t}=\Omega_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}a}
. Let
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,b)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a weak conformal Willmore immersion. Define

\displaystyle E=\left\|\nabla\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}dx.

For all
0<\beta<1
there exists
\varepsilon_{1}(\beta,n)>0
and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C_{k}(E,n)<\infty
such that the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\varepsilon_{1}(\beta,n)

(3.134)

implies that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}
,

\displaystyle|z|^{k}|\nabla^{k}\vec{n}(z)|\leq C_{k}(E,n)\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.135)

Proof.

Assuming that
\varepsilon_{1}(\beta,n)>0
is small enough, we deduce by
[
46
, Theorem C, (
1.5
)]
that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C_{1}e^{C_{1}E}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

while Theorem
3.25
shows (up to worsening the constants) that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/4}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}(0))}\leq C\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{\Lambda}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\right).

We need only control

\displaystyle\Lambda=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega_{1/4}}\frac{|\nabla\vec{n}|}{|x|}dx.

As previously, the
L^{2,1}/L^{2,\infty}
duality implies that

\displaystyle\Lambda\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

Finally, thanks to the
\varepsilon
-regularity, we deduce that for some universal constant such that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/8}
,

\displaystyle|z||\nabla\vec{n}(z)|\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}(0))}\leq C\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

the conclusion follows by replacing dyadic annuli by smaller annuli and worsening the constants.
∎

3.6.2
Argument in Arbitrary Codimension

Thanks to the analysis of
[
55
]
(see also
[
20
, Lemme
4.1.3
]
), we deduce that the exists a trivialisation
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\cdots\wedge\vec{n}_{n-2}
satisfying for all
1\leq i,j\leq n-2
the Coulomb condition

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \dive\left(\nabla\vec{n}_{i}\cdot\vec{n}_{j}\right)&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\partial_{\nu}\vec{n}_{i}\cdot\vec{n}_{j}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.136)

where

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Furthermore, for all
1\leq i\leq n-2
, the following pointwise identity holds

\displaystyle\nabla\vec{n}_{i}=-\ast(\vec{n}\wedge\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{i})+\sum_{j=1}^{n-2}\langle\nabla\vec{n}_{i},\vec{n}_{j}\rangle\,\vec{n}_{j}-2\,H_{i}\nabla\vec{\Phi},

(3.137)

where we wrote
\displaystyle\vec{H}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}H_{i}\,\vec{n}_{i}
.
Thanks to the Coulomb condition (
3.136
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Delta\vec{n}_{i}=-\ast\left(\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\wedge\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{i}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{n-2}\left\langle\nabla\vec{n}_{i},\vec{n}_{j}\right\rangle\cdot\nabla\vec{n}_{j}-2\,\dive\left(H_{i}\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{k}\right).

Theorem 3.30
.

Let
n\geq 3
,
0<4\,a<b<\infty
,
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, and for all
0<t\leq 1
, defined
\Omega_{t}=\Omega_{t\,b}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}a}
. Let
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,b)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a weak conformal Willmore immersion. Define

\displaystyle E=\left\|\nabla\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}dx.

For all
0<\beta<1
there exists
\varepsilon_{1}(\beta,n)>0
and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C_{k}(E,n)<\infty
such that the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\varepsilon_{1}(\beta,n)

(3.138)

implies that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}
,

\displaystyle|z|^{k}|\nabla^{k}\vec{n}(z)|\leq C_{k}(E,n)\left(\left(\frac{|z|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|z|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{b}{4a}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.139)

Proof.

The proof is the same as the one of Theorem
3.29
and we skip it.
∎

4
Proof of the Main Theorem for Precompact Conformal Class

In this section, we will prove a version of
[
31
, Lemma IV.
2
]
in our setting. Fix some
\frac{1}{2}<\beta<1
and introduce the following weight on
\Sigma
:

\displaystyle\omega_{a,b}(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{|x|^{4m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\\
&\frac{1}{b^{4m}}\left(1+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,a)\\
&\frac{1}{a^{4m}}\left(\left(1+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,a)\end{aligned}\right.

(4.1)

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
0<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
be such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{2}<\beta_{1}<1\\
&\sqrt{2}-1<\beta_{2}<1\end{aligned}\right.

(4.2)

and
\{\vec{\Phi}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of Willmore immersions from Theorem
A
, and let
\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}(0)
be a neck region. Then, there exists
\lambda_{1}(\beta_{1}),\lambda_{2}(\beta_{2})>0
and
\alpha_{0}>0
such that for all
0<\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
large and for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{1}(\beta_{1})\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

(4.3)

\displaystyle+\lambda_{2}(\beta_{2})\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(4.4)

Proof.

For technical reasons, we first let
0<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
be such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{2}<\beta_{1}<1-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\\
&\sqrt{2}-1<\beta_{2}=2\beta_{1}-2+\sqrt{2}<1\end{aligned}\right.

(4.5)

Since we have assumed that the limiting map and the bubbles were immersions, we deduce by the main theorem of
[
46
]
that there exists
\alpha_{0}>0
such that for all

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda_{k}}=e^{2\mu_{k}},

(4.6)

where
\mu_{k}\in C^{0}(\Omega_{k}(B(0,\alpha_{0}))
and for all
0<\alpha<\alpha_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,\alpha))}+\left\|\mu_{k}-c_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}\leq C(n)\left(\sqrt{\alpha}\left\|\nabla\lambda_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}+\int_{\Omega_{k}(2\alpha)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}|^{2}dx\right).

(4.7)

where
c_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}c\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, using the two main estimates from
[
47
]
, we deduce that for all
1/2<\beta_{1}<1
and for all
\sqrt{2}-1<\beta_{2}<1
there exists
0<C_{\beta_{1},1},C_{\beta_{2},2}<\infty
such that for all
u\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\Delta u)^{2}dx

\displaystyle\geq C_{\beta_{1},1}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle+C_{\beta_{2},2}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(4.8)

Then, using inequality (
2.4
), we deduce that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-2\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla u|^{2}|A_{k}|^{2}dx-2\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle-4\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla u||u||\nabla H_{k}||A_{k}|dx.

Furthermore, thanks to estimates (
4.6
) and (
4.7
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}e^{-2\mu_{k}}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq\frac{1}{2}e^{-2c}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\Delta u)^{2}dx

for
k
large enough. Therefore we deduce that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}(u)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{8}e^{-2c}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\Delta u)^{2}dx-2\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla u|^{2}|A_{k}|^{2}dx-2\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle-4\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla u||u||\nabla H_{k}||A_{k}|dx.

(4.9)

Then, Theorem
3.30
shows that there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
(depending only on
\beta
and
n
) such that

\displaystyle|A_{k}(x)|\leq\frac{C}{|x|}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}.

(4.10)

Therefore, we get (by the elementary inequality
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 4a^{2}+4b^{2}+2c^{2}\leq 4(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})

\displaystyle 2\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla u|^{2}|A_{k}|^{2}dx\leq 4C^{2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(4.11)

On the other hand, we have for
k
large enough

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda_{k}}|A_{k}|^{4}\leq 32e^{2c}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{4}\frac{C^{4}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)dx,

which implies that (by the inequality
(a+b+c)^{4}\leq 16(a^{4}+b^{4}+c^{4})
)

\displaystyle 2\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|A_{k}|^{2}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\leq 64e^{2c}C^{2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{4}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)^{4\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(4.12)

Then, we have for all
1/2<\beta<1

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla u||u||\nabla H_{k}||A_{k}|dx

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{\beta}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|}\frac{|u|}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(4.13)

We trivially estimate by virtue of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|}\frac{|u|}{|x|^{2}}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}dx\leq\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.14)

Then, we have for all
0<\varepsilon<1

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|}\frac{|u|}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}\right)dx\leq 2\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\varepsilon\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\beta}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4(1-\varepsilon)\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4(1-\varepsilon)\beta}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.15)

In order to control this quantity thanks to (
4
), we must choose
1/2<\beta<1
and
0<\varepsilon<1
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} 4\varepsilon\beta>2(\sqrt{2}-1)\\
4(1-\varepsilon)\beta>2.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.16)

Summing both equations, we deduce that
\beta>\dfrac{\sqrt{2}}{2}=0.707\cdots
. Then, we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \varepsilon>\frac{\sqrt{2}-1}{2\beta}\\
\varepsilon<1-\frac{1}{2\beta}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.17)

Therefore, we must have

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}-1}{2\beta}<1-\frac{1}{2\beta},

which is indeed equivalent to
\beta>\dfrac{\sqrt{2}}{2}
. For such a
\beta
, there exists
0<\varepsilon<1
solving (
4.16
). Explicitly, we can choose

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}-1}{2\beta}+1-\frac{1}{2\beta}\right)=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{(2-\sqrt{2})}{4\beta}.

Then, we get

\displaystyle 2\beta_{2}=4\varepsilon\beta=2\beta-(2-\sqrt{2})>2(\sqrt{2}-1)

(4.18)

since we have assume that
\beta>\dfrac{\sqrt{2}}{2}
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle 4\beta_{1}=4(1-\varepsilon)\beta=2\beta+2-\sqrt{2}>2

(4.19)

since
\beta>\dfrac{\sqrt{2}}{2}
. Gathering (
4
), (
4.14
), and (
4
), we deduce that

\displaystyle 4\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla u||u||\nabla H_{k}||A_{k}|dx

\displaystyle\leq 4C_{\beta}^{\prime}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle+4C_{\beta}^{\prime}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

(4.20)

Therefore, if
0<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
are chosen as in the statement of the theorem, we deduce by (
4
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
), and (
4
) that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)\geq\left(\frac{1}{8}e^{-2c}C_{\beta_{1},1}-\left(4C_{\beta}^{\prime}+64e^{2c}C^{2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{8}e^{-2c}C_{\beta_{2},2}-4\left(C^{2}+C_{\beta}^{\prime}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}\right)\int_{\Omega_{k}(2\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)dx,

where we assumed without loss of generality that
\delta_{k}\leq\alpha^{2}e
in order to get the trivial estimate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\leq\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}.

Therefore, the quantization of energy (
[
5
]
)

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}=0

completes the proof of the theorem. Now, if
1\leq\beta_{1}\leq\beta<1
, then

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\leq\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}.

Therefore, coming back to (
4.16
), we see that as
\varepsilon,\beta>0
varies,
\beta_{1}
or
\beta_{2}
can be chosen arbitrarily (as long as (
4.2
) is satisfied). Therefore, we deduce that (
4.3
) holds for all
0<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
satisfying the conditions in (
4.2
).
∎

Now, recall the formula

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g}u+|A|^{2}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du|_{g}^{2}+4|h_{0}|_{WP}^{2}u^{2}\right)H^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0}\rangle_{WP}H\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(4.21)

We want to introduce two weights, both adapted to derivatives of different order.

\displaystyle\omega_{1,\alpha,k}(x)=\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)

and

\displaystyle\omega_{2,k}(x)=\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right).

We introduce a third weight

\displaystyle\omega_{3,k}=\sqrt{\omega_{1,k}\omega_{2,k}}.

This is justified in the proof of Theorem
4.1
since
\beta
is given thanks to (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) by

\displaystyle\beta=\beta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{1},

so that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}=\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{1}}\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{2}}=\sqrt{\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}}

and shows that this is the weight that appeared in the proof of Theorem
4.1
. Then, we write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,\Delta_{g}^{2}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left((\omega_{1,k}+\omega_{2,k})^{-1}\Delta_{g}^{2}u\right)u(\omega_{1,k}+\omega_{2,k})d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

For the next term, we write

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|A|^{2}u\,\Delta_{g}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\int_{\Sigma}(\omega_{1,k}^{-1}|A|^{2}\Delta_{g}u)u\,\omega_{1,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

The next term is

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g}^{2}H^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\int_{\Sigma}u\,d^{\ast_{g}}(H^{2}\,du)d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\int_{\Sigma}\omega_{2,k}^{-1}d^{\ast_{g}}\left(H^{2}\,du\right)u\,\omega_{2,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

We then rewrite

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}4|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}H^{2}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=\int_{\Sigma}\left(4\,\omega_{1,k}^{-1}|h_{0}|_{WP}^{2}H^{2}u\right)u\,\omega_{1,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

The last two terms are rewritten the following way

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=8\int_{\Sigma}\omega_{2,k}^{-1}\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(H\,g^{-1}\otimes\vec{h}_{0}\otimes\partial u\right)u\,\omega_{2,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

and

\displaystyle-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}=-16\int_{\Sigma}\left(\omega_{3,k}^{-1}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)u\,\omega_{3,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Now, we write thanks to (
2.3
)

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,k}u\right)u\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}},

(4.22)

where
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,k}=\omega_{1,\alpha,k}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}
, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{g_{k}}^{2}+|A_{k}|^{2}\Delta_{g_{k}}+2\langle d|A_{k}|^{2},d(\,\cdot\,)\rangle_{g_{k}}+2\,d^{\ast_{g_{k}}}\left(H_{k}^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)+\left(|A_{k}|^{4}+\Delta_{g}|A_{k}|^{2}+24|H_{k}|^{2}|h_{0,k}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+16\,\ast_{g_{k}}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g_{k}^{-1}\otimes h_{0,k}\otimes\partial(H_{k}\,\cdot\,)\right)-16\langle\partial(\,\cdot\,)\otimes\partial H_{k},h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

Thanks to (
4.22
), we deduce that
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,k}
is a self-adjoint operator with respect to the weight
\omega_{1,\alpha,k}
. Then, we generalise
[
31
, Lemma IV.
3
]
to out setting.

Lemma 4.2
.

For all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda)=W^{2,2}(\Sigma)\cap\left\{u:\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,k}u=\lambda\,u\right\}.

(4.23)

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{k})=\dim\bigoplus_{\lambda<0}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda).

Proof.

The proof is identical as the one in
[
31
]
and we omit it.
∎

Now, recall the following theorem from
[
47
, Theorem
4.5
]
, which is a generalisation of the afore-mentioned estimate for functions that do
not
necessarily vanish on the boundary.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
and
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
. For all
\dfrac{1}{2}<\beta<1
and
\sqrt{2}-1<\gamma<1
there exists a universal constant
C_{\beta,\gamma}<\infty
such that
for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega)
, provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\max\left\{2,\frac{1}{2\beta-1}\log\left(4\beta\right),\frac{1}{4\beta}\log\left(\frac{2}{2-\sqrt{3}}\right),\frac{1}{4(1-\beta)}\log\left(1+\frac{8\beta(1-\beta)}{(2\beta-1)^{2}}\right),\frac{1}{4\beta}\log(8\beta(\beta+1))\right\},

(4.24)

we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{\beta,\gamma}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx\right).

(4.25)

The generalisation of the next result (
[
31
, Lemma IV.
4
]
) will be considerably more involved for it makes use of Theorem
4.3
.

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists
\alpha_{0}>0
and
\mu_{0}>0
and a family of constants
\left\{\mu_{\alpha,k}\right\}_{0<\alpha<\alpha_{0},k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\mu_{0})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mu_{\alpha,k}=0.

Furthermore, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}\,\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda)>0\implies\lambda\geq-\mu_{\alpha,k}\geq-\mu_{0}.

Proof.

First, we let
\dfrac{1}{2}<\beta_{1}<1
and
\sqrt{2}-1<\beta_{2}<1
to be defined later.
Write
\omega_{\alpha,k}=\omega_{1,\alpha,k}
for simplicity. We have

\displaystyle\omega_{1,\alpha,k}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{4}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}(0)\\
&\frac{1}{\alpha^{4}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}),\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\omega_{2,\alpha,k}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}(0)\\
&\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}).\end{aligned}\right.

First, using (
4.10
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}=0.

(4.26)

Assume without loss of generality that there are no bubbles in
\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)
. Then, we have on
\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)

\displaystyle\omega_{\alpha,\infty}=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\omega_{1,\alpha,k}=\frac{1}{\alpha^{4}}.

Thanks to the strong convergence on
\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)
and the absence of branch point at the limit (which implies that
A_{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma)
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha}(0))}=\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\alpha\left\|A_{\infty}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha))}=0.

(4.27)

Likewise, the strong convergence of the bubble (and the absence of branch point of the bubble at the point of concentration) finally shows by (
4.26
) and (
4.27
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}=0.

(4.28)

Then, we introduce on
\Sigma
the weight

\displaystyle\omega_{\alpha,\infty}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{4}}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha).\end{aligned}\right.

Notice that since
\beta_{1}>\dfrac{1}{2}
, there exists
p>1
such that
\omega_{\alpha,\infty}\in L^{p}(\Sigma)
.
Previous estimate (
4.27
) shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{A_{\infty}}{\omega_{\infty}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha}(0))}=\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha))}=O(\alpha).

(4.29)

On the other hand, since
|\mathring{A}_{k}|^{2}e^{2\lambda_{k}}
is a pointwise conformal invariant, and
\left\{\lambda_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(\Sigma)
due to the absence of branch points, we deduce that we can assume that

\displaystyle\vec{\Psi}_{k}(z)=\vec{\Phi}_{k}(\delta_{k}z)\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}\quad\text{in}\;\,C^{l}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\widehat{\mathbb{C}})\;\,\text{for all}\;\,l\in\mathbb{N}.

Then, we get

\displaystyle A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)=\delta_{k}^{-1}A_{k}(\delta_{k}x).

Now, recall that on
B(0,\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k})
, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{k}(x)=\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k})^{4}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right).

In particular, we have for all
x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle\frac{A_{k}(\delta_{k}x)}{\omega_{k}(\delta_{k}x)^{\frac{1}{4}}}=\frac{\delta_{k}A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)}{(\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k})}\left(1+\left(\frac{\delta_{k}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\alpha A_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}(x),

(4.30)

which implies that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}))}=\alpha\left\|A_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}))}=O(\alpha)

(4.31)

since
A_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}\in L^{\infty}(S^{2})
by hypothesis since
\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}
is an immersion (which allows us to apply the classical
\varepsilon
-regularity
[
55
]
).
Therefore, using the three inequalities (
4.26
), (
4.27
), and (
4.29
), defining

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,k}=\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)},

(4.32)

we deduce that the two conditions below hold for some
\alpha_{0}>0
and some
\mu_{0}<\infty

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mu_{\alpha,k}=0,\\
&\text{For all}\;\,k\in\mathbb{N},\;\,\text{for all}\;\,0<\alpha<\alpha_{0},\;\,\text{we have}\;\,0<\mu_{\alpha,k}<\mu_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.33)

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and assume that

\displaystyle\dim\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda)>0,

where we recall that
\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda)
is defined in (
4.23
).
Then, there exists
u\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)\setminus\left\{0\right\}
such that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)=\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}},

or

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u+|A_{k}|^{2}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}+4|h_{0,k}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}u^{2}\right)H_{k}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H_{k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H_{k},h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

(4.34)

Now, let us prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}\nabla A_{k}}{\sqrt{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}=0.

This estimate holds in
\Omega_{k}(\alpha)
thanks to the proof of Theorem
4.1
, provided that
\dfrac{\sqrt{2}}{2}<\beta<1
and
0<\varepsilon<1
satisfy the conditions (
4.16
). Likewise, thanks to the strong convergence on
\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}\nabla A_{k}}{\sqrt{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}=\left\|\frac{A_{\infty}\nabla A_{\infty}}{\sqrt{\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}\omega_{2,\alpha,\infty}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha))}.

On
\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)
, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{2,\alpha,\infty}(x)=\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}},

which implies since
A_{\infty}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{A_{\infty}\nabla A_{\infty}}{\sqrt{\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}\omega_{2,\alpha,\infty}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha))}=O(\alpha^{3}).

Then, on the bubble, we have

\displaystyle\nabla A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)=\delta_{k}^{-2}\nabla A_{k}(\delta_{k}x),

while

\displaystyle\sqrt{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}}=\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k})^{3}}\sqrt{1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}}\sqrt{1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}},

which implies that for all
x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1})
,

\displaystyle\frac{A_{k}(\delta_{k}x)\nabla A_{k}(\delta_{k}x)}{\sqrt{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}(\delta_{k}x)\omega_{2,\alpha,k}(\delta_{k}x)}}=\alpha^{3}A_{\vec{\Psi_{k}}}(x)\nabla A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\alpha^{3}A_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}(x)\nabla A_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}(x)=O(\alpha^{3}).

Therefore, if

\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha,k}=\left\|\frac{A_{k}\nabla A_{k}}{\sqrt{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)},

(4.35)

we deduce that the two conditions below hold for some
\alpha_{0}>0
and some
\nu_{0}<\infty

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\nu_{\alpha,k}=0,\\
&\text{For all}\;\,k\in\mathbb{N},\;\,\text{for all}\;\,0<\alpha<\alpha_{0},\;\,\text{we have}\;\,0<\nu_{\alpha,k}<\nu_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.36)

Then, we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H_{k},h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\,u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq\nu_{\alpha,k}\left(\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.37)

Since
\omega_{2,\alpha,k}
is constant and Lipschitzian on
\Sigma\setminus\Omega_{k}(\alpha)
, integrating by parts, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\,\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=-\int_{\Sigma}u\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)\,\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u\,\nabla u\cdot\nabla\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,dx.

(4.38)

We first estimate thanks to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}u\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)\,\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq\left(\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{2,\alpha,k}^{2}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.39)

Then, if we choose
\beta_{2}\geq\beta_{1}
, we deduce by the elementary inequality
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 4(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})
that

\displaystyle\omega_{2,\alpha,k}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}\right)\leq 4\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\qquad\text{in}\;\,\Omega_{k}(\alpha).

The same inequality on
\Sigma\setminus\overline{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}
implies that

\displaystyle\omega_{2,\alpha,k}^{2}\leq 4\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\qquad\text{in}\;\,\Sigma.

(4.40)

Therefore, (
4.38
) shows that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}u\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)\,\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq 2\left(\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.41)

Now, we have

\displaystyle\nabla\omega_{2,\alpha,k}=-2\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}+2\beta_{2}\frac{x}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}-\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla\omega_{2,\alpha,k}|\leq\frac{2(1+\beta_{2})}{|x|}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}.

We first have by Cauchy’s inequality

\displaystyle 2(1+\beta_{2})\left|\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u\,\nabla u\cdot\frac{x}{|x|^{4}}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha}{\delta_{k}}\right)}dx\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}dx

\displaystyle+2(1+\beta_{2})^{2}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\delta_{k}}\right)}dx\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\,\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+C\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

(4.42)

Then, using inequality (
4
) (where
\beta
is replaced by
\beta_{2}
), provided that
\beta_{2}>\dfrac{\sqrt{2}}{2}
, there exists
\dfrac{1}{2}<\beta_{1}^{\prime}<1
and
\sqrt{2}-1<\beta_{2}^{\prime}<1
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|}\frac{|u|}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}\right)dx\leq 2\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}^{\prime}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}^{\prime}}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}^{\prime}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}^{\prime}}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.43)

Furthermore, the chosen
0<\varepsilon<1
in (
4.16
) is such that
\varepsilon<\dfrac{1}{2}
(which is in fact trivial since
\beta_{2}<1
and
4\beta_{1}^{\prime}=4(1-\varepsilon)\beta_{2}>2
). In particular, we have
\beta_{1}^{\prime}\geq\beta_{2}\geq\beta_{1}
, so by the elementary inequality

\displaystyle x^{4\beta_{1}^{\prime}}\leq x^{4\beta_{1}}\quad\text{for all}\;\,0<x<1,

we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta_{1}^{\prime}}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}^{\prime}}\right)dx\leq C\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

(4.44)

Now, this is where the weighted Poincaré estimate comes in, since
\beta_{2}^{\prime}<\beta_{2}
, which prevents one from controlling the associated weighted integral by the initial integral. Thanks to Theorem
4.3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\beta_{2}^{\prime}}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}^{\prime}}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}\right)dx

\displaystyle+C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}^{\prime}}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx.

(4.45)

Now, by the uniform boundedness of
\left\{g_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
(see
[
1
, Remark
2.36
p.
56
]
for example), we deduce that that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx\leq\int_{\Sigma}|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|^{2}_{g_{0}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{0}}\leq C\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}},

where
g_{0}
is any smooth metric chosen flat on
\Omega_{k}(\alpha)
, and
C<\infty
is independent of
\alpha>0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, (
4
), (
4.44
), and (
4
) show that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|}\frac{|u|}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}\right)dx\leq C\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.46)

We deduce by (
4.38
), (
4.41
), (
4
), and (
4
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\,\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+C\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and finally

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+C\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.47)

where we used the fact that
\left\{g_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a uniformly bounded metric. Therefore, we get by (
4.37
) and (
4.47
)

\displaystyle 16\left|\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H_{k},h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\,\nu_{\alpha,k}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\,\nu_{\alpha,k}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}},

(4.48)

and if
\kappa_{\alpha,k}
is defined like
\mu_{\alpha,k}
but with
\beta_{1}
replaced by
\beta_{2}
, we get by (
4.47
)

\displaystyle 16\left|\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H_{k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq\kappa_{\alpha,k}^{2}\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\,\kappa_{\alpha,k}^{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\,\kappa_{\alpha,k}^{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

(4.49)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u+|A_{k}|^{2}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-\int_{\Sigma}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-\mu_{\alpha,k}^{4}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

(4.50)

Therefore, by (
4
), (
4.48
), (
4
), and (
4
), we deduce that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}-C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\left(\nu_{\alpha,k}+\kappa_{\alpha,k}^{2}+\mu_{\alpha,k}^{4}\right)\right)\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle-C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\left(\nu_{\alpha,k}+\kappa_{\alpha,k}^{2}+\mu_{\alpha,k}^{4}\right)\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

(4.51)

Now, let
\alpha_{0}>0
be such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\nu_{\alpha_{0},k}+\kappa_{\alpha_{0},k}^{2}+\mu_{\alpha_{0},k}^{4}\right)<\frac{1}{4}\frac{1}{C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}}.

Then, for all
0<\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
, if (
4
) holds, we get for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
large enough by (
4
)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)\geq-C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\left(\nu_{\alpha,k}+\kappa_{\alpha,k}^{2}+\mu_{\alpha,k}^{4}\right)\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}},

(4.52)

which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

We now generalise
[
31
, Lemma B.
1
]
to our setting.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a smooth Willmore immersion. Let
p\in\Sigma
, let
\omega\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\left\{p\right\})
such that for some
\dfrac{1}{2}<\beta<1
,

\displaystyle 0\leq\omega_{0}<\omega(x)\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\mathrm{dist}(x,p)^{4(1-\beta)}}\quad\text{and}\quad|\nabla\omega_{0}(x)|\leq\frac{C_{l}}{\mathrm{dist}(x,p)^{4(1-\beta)+l}}\;\,\text{for all}\;\,0\leq l\in\mathbb{N},

(4.53)

where
\left\{C_{l}\right\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty)
. Denote

\displaystyle L^{2}_{\omega}(\Sigma)=L^{2}(\Sigma)\cap\left\{u:\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{0}}<\infty\right\},

(4.54)

where
g_{0}
is any smooth metric on
\Sigma
. If
g=\vec{\Phi}^{\ast}g_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
is the induced metric on
\Sigma
. Consider the operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g,\omega}

\displaystyle=\omega^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{g}=\omega^{-1}\left(\Delta_{g}^{2}+|A|^{2}\Delta_{g}+2\langle d|A|^{2},d(\,\cdot\,)\rangle_{g}+2\,d^{\ast_{g}}\left(H^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(|A|^{4}+\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}+24\,H^{2}|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)+16\,\ast_{g}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes h_{0}\otimes\partial(H\,\cdot\,)\right)-16\langle\partial(\,\cdot\,)\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)

acting on
W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
functions. Then, there exists a Hilbertian base of
L^{2}_{\omega}(\Sigma)
made of eigenvectors of
\mathcal{L}_{g,\omega}
whose eigenvalues satisfy

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}\leq\cdots\leq\lambda_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\infty.

Remark 4.6
.

Notice that the definition of the weighted
L^{2}
space
L^{2}_{\omega}
does not depend on the choice of the metric
g_{0}
on
\Sigma
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Theorem
2.6
. First, as
\vec{\Phi}
is smooth,
\mathscr{L}_{g,\omega}u
is a well-defined distribution for all
u\in L^{2}_{\omega}(\Sigma)
. For all
\lambda>0
, let
\mathcal{L}_{g,\omega}^{\lambda}=\mathcal{L}_{g,\omega}+\lambda\mathrm{Id}
. Then, let
f\in L^{2}_{\omega}(\Sigma)
and
u\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
is such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g,\omega}^{\lambda}u=f.

Now, define the bilinear form
B_{\lambda}
on
W^{2,2}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}(u,v)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,\mathcal{L}_{g}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,v\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

and let
Q_{\lambda}(u)=B_{\lambda}(u,u)
.
Recalling (
2.60
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}f\,u\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\geq\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}+10|A|^{4}-8\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right)u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

Therefore, taking
\lambda>\lambda_{0}=2\left\|\Delta_{g}|A|^{2}+10|A|^{4}-8\langle\nabla^{2}_{\partial,\partial}H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}
, we deduce by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\frac{\lambda-\lambda_{0}}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\leq\left(\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}f^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda-\lambda_{0}}{4}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\frac{4}{\lambda-\lambda_{0}}\int_{\Sigma}f^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

which finally implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\frac{\lambda-\lambda_{0}}{4}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}\leq\frac{4}{\lambda-\lambda_{0}}\int_{\Sigma}f^{2}\,\omega\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g},

The rest of the proof is exactly the same as the one of Theorem
2.6
and we omit it.
∎

Now, introduce as in
[
31
]

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,\infty}^{0}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\leq 0}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,\infty}(\lambda),

where
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,\infty}^{0}=\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{g_{\vec{\Phi}_{\infty}}}
, and
\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,\infty}(\lambda)
is defined as in (
4.23
).

Then, we also get

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}(\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,\infty})\leq\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{\infty})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{\infty}).

We can finally move to the proof of the main theorem.

Proof.

Consider the finite-dimensional sphere

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{\alpha,k}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\leq 0}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda)\cap\left\{u:\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=1\right\}.

(4.55)

Let
u_{k}\in\mathscr{S}_{\alpha,k}
. Thanks to (
4
), for
k
large enough, we have

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u_{k})\geq\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u_{k})^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-\mu_{\alpha,k}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}},

while for some
\lambda>-\mu_{\alpha,k}\geq-\mu_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u_{k})=\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u_{k}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\geq-\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u_{k})^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}.

Then, since
\Sigma
is closed, we have by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du_{k}|_{g_{k}}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Sigma}u_{k}\,\Delta_{g_{k}}u_{k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\leq\left(\int_{\Sigma}u_{k}^{2}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u_{k})^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\mu_{0}.

Therefore,
\left\{u_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
. Up to a subsequence, we deduce that

\displaystyle u_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\hookrightarrow}u_{\infty}\quad\text{in}\;\,W^{2,2}(\Sigma)\qquad\text{and}\qquad u_{k}(\delta_{k}z)\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\hookrightarrow}v_{\infty}\quad\text{in}\;\,W^{2,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{C}).

Claim 1:
u_{\infty}\neq 0
or
v_{\infty}\neq 0
.

Let
\left\{\varphi_{k,j}\right\}_{1\leq j\leq N_{k}}
be an orthonormal base of

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}^{0}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\leq 0}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda).

For all
1\leq j\leq N_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}\varphi_{k,j}=\lambda_{k,j}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,\varphi_{k,j}.

Since
u_{k}\in\mathscr{S}_{\alpha,k}\simeq S^{N_{k}-1}
, we have a decomposition

\displaystyle u_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}a_{k,j}\varphi_{k,j},

where
a=\left\{a_{k,j}\right\}_{1\leq j\leq N_{k}}\in S^{N_{k}-1}
. Then, we get as
-\mu_{0}\leq\lambda_{k,j}\leq 0
the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\frac{1}{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}}|\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}u_{k}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N_{k}}a_{k,i}a_{k,j}\lambda_{k,i}\lambda_{k,j}\varphi_{k,i}\varphi_{k,j}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}|a_{k,j}|^{2}|\lambda_{k,j}|^{2}\leq\mu_{0}^{2}.

Therefore, there exists a uniformly bounded sequence of functions
\left\{f_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^{2}(\Sigma)
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}u_{k}=\sqrt{\omega_{\alpha,k}}f_{k}.

By classical elliptic estimates, as
\left\{g_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded and smooth, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{4}u_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha))}\leq C_{\alpha}.

Likewise, making a linear change of variable, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{4}v_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}))}\leq C_{\alpha},

where
v_{k}(x)=u_{k}(\rho_{k}x)
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle u_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\rightharpoonup}u_{\infty}\quad\text{in}\;\,W^{4,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Sigma\setminus\left\{0\right\})\quad\text{and}\quad v_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\rightharpoonup}v_{\infty}\quad\text{in}\;\,W^{4,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{C}).

(4.56)

Assume by contradiction that
u_{\infty}=0
and
v_{\infty}=0
. Let
\chi_{\alpha,k}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
be a cutoff function such that
\chi_{\alpha,k}=1
in
\Sigma\setminus\overline{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}
and
\chi_{\alpha,k}=0
in
\Omega_{k}(\alpha/2)
and
\widetilde{u}_{k}=\chi_{\alpha,k}u_{k}
. We can also assume that for some universal constant
C<\infty
independent of
\alpha
and
k
, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla^{l}\chi_{\alpha,k}|\leq\frac{C}{|x|^{l}}\quad\text{for all}\;\,x\in\left(B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha/2}(0)\right)\cup\;\,\left(B_{2\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}(0)\right)\text{for all}\;\,l=0,1,2.

First, we trivially get by the contradiction hypothesis and the strong convergence that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}u_{k}(1-\chi_{\alpha},k)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Sigma)}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}0.

Then, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du_{k}|_{g_{k}}|^{2}|d\chi_{\alpha,k}|_{g_{k}}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\int_{B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx+C^{2}\int_{B_{2\alpha^{-1}}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}}(0)}\frac{|\nabla v_{k}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}C^{2}\int_{B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u_{\infty}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx+C^{2}\int_{B_{2\alpha^{-1}}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}}(0)}\frac{|\nabla v_{\infty}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=0

by assumption. Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\Sigma}u_{k}^{2}|\nabla^{2}\chi_{\alpha,k}|^{2}_{g_{k}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\leq C^{2}\int_{B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(0)}\frac{u_{\infty}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx+C^{2}\int_{B_{2\alpha^{-1}}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}}(0)}\frac{v_{\infty}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=0.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|u_{k}-\widetilde{u}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{2,2}(\Sigma)}=0.

(4.57)

Now, recall the formula

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u_{k}+|A_{k}|^{2}u_{k}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du_{k}|_{g_{k}}^{2}+4|h_{0,k}|_{WP}^{2}u_{k}^{2}\right)H_{k}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u_{k}\otimes\partial u_{k},h_{0,k}\rangle_{WP}H_{k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u_{k}\otimes\partial H_{k},h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u_{k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

Thanks to the boundedness of
\left\{g_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\left\{A_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, we deduce by (
4.57
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}|Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u_{k})-Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(\widetilde{u}_{k})|=0.

(4.58)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}u_{k}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-\int_{\Sigma}\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq\int_{B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(0)}u_{k}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+\int_{B_{2\alpha^{-1}}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(0)}v_{k}^{2}\,\widetilde{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}}

\displaystyle\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\int_{B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(0)}u_{\infty}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\infty}}+\int_{B_{2\alpha^{-1}}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(0)}v_{\infty}^{2}\,\frac{1}{\alpha^{4}}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}}=0.

Thanks to the normalisation of
u_{k}\in\mathscr{S}_{\alpha,k}
(see (
4.55
)), we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\Sigma}\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=1.

(4.59)

Therefore, we deduce by Theorem
4.1
, (
4.59
), and the boundedness of the sequence of metrics
\left\{g_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(\widetilde{u}_{k})\geq\lambda_{1}(\beta_{1})\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\lambda_{1}(\beta_{1})>0.

However, this contradicts the fact that
Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u_{k})\leq 0
due to the convergence in (
4.58
).

Therefore, we deduce that either
u_{\infty}\neq 0
or
v_{\infty}\neq 0
and
Claim 1
is proven.

End of the proof of the main theorem.
As in the proof of
Claim 1
, let
\left\{\varphi_{k,j}\right\}_{1\leq j\leq N_{k}}
be an orthonormal base of

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\lambda\leq 0}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda),

(4.60)

and let
\left\{\lambda_{k,j}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N},j\in\left\{1,\cdots,N_{k}\right\}}
be such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}\varphi_{k,j}=\lambda_{k,j}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\,\varphi_{k,j}.

Thanks to (
4.56
), for all
1\leq j\leq N=\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}N_{k}
we deduce that there exists
\lambda_{\infty,j}\leq 0
and
\varphi_{\infty,j}\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
and
\psi_{\infty,j}\in W^{2,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{C})
such that
