\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}_{g_{\infty}}\varphi_{\infty,j}&&=\lambda_{\infty,j}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}\,\varphi_{\infty,j}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Sigma\\
&\mathcal{L}_{g_{\Psi_{\infty}}}\psi_{\infty,j}&&=\lambda_{\infty,j}\,\widehat{\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}}\,\psi_{\infty,j}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\mathbb{C},\end{aligned}\right.

(4.61)

where

\displaystyle\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{|x|^{4(1-\beta)}}\frac{1}{\alpha^{4\beta}}\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{\alpha^{4}}\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}}(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{|x|^{4(1-\beta)}}\frac{1}{\alpha^{4\beta}}\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha^{-1})\\
&\frac{1}{\alpha^{4}}\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}).\end{aligned}\right.

Indeed, since the branch point is of multiplicity
1
, there exists a bounded sequence
\left\{\mu_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for all
\alpha>0
small enough

\displaystyle\vec{\Psi}_{k}(z)=e^{-\mu_{k}}\vec{\Phi}_{k}(\rho_{k}\,z)\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}(z)\quad\text{for all}\;\,C^{l}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}))\;\,\text{for all}\;\,l\in\mathbb{N}.

Furthermore, an elementary computation shows that for all
z\in B(0,\alpha^{-1})
,

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(z)}=\rho_{k}^{2}e^{-2\mu_{k}}e^{2\lambda_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(\rho_{k}z)}.

Therefore, if
\psi_{k,j}(z)=e^{\mu_{k}}\varphi_{k,j}(\rho_{k}\,z)
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}\varphi_{k,j}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=\int_{B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\left(\Delta\varphi_{k,j}(w)\right)^{2}e^{-2\lambda_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(w)}|dw|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\left(\rho_{k}^{-2}\Delta\psi_{k,j}(\rho_{k}^{-1}w)\right)^{2}\rho_{k}^{2}e^{2\lambda_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(\rho_{k}^{-1}w)}|dw|^{2}=\int_{B(0,\alpha^{-1})}\left(\Delta_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}}\psi_{k,j}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}},

which shows by the weak convergence that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,\alpha^{-1})}\left(\Delta_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}}\psi_{\infty,j}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}}\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}\varphi_{k,j}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}<\infty.

(4.62)

Since all terms in the second derivative enjoy the same scaling, we deduce that
\psi_{\infty,j}\in W^{2,2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Furthermore, if our limiting immersion is parametrised by
\mathbb{C}
, and
\pi:S^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the stereographic projection, by conformal invariance of the Willmore energy, if
\widetilde{\vec{\Psi}}_{\infty}=\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}\circ\pi:S^{2}\setminus\left\{N\right\}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
, then

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}(\widetilde{\Psi}_{\infty})=\mathscr{W}({\Psi}_{\infty}),

where for all
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}(\vec{\Phi})=\int_{\Sigma}\left(|\vec{H}|^{2}-K_{g}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

If
\widetilde{\vec{\Psi}}_{\infty}:S^{2}\setminus{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
extend to a continuous function on
S^{2}
, and
\psi_{\infty,j}
extends to a
W^{2,2}(S^{2})
function. Otherwise, making an inversion
\vec{\chi}_{\infty}=\dfrac{\widetilde{\vec{\Psi}}_{\infty}}{|\widetilde{\vec{\Psi}}_{\infty}|^{2}}:S^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we deduce by
[
39
]
(see also
[
41
]
) that for all
v\in W^{2,2}(S^{2})

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\chi}_{\infty}}(v)=Q_{\widetilde{\Psi}_{\infty}}\left(|\widetilde{\vec{\Psi}}_{\infty}|^{2}v\right).

(4.63)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\chi}_{\infty})=\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\widetilde{\vec{\Psi}}_{\infty})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\chi}_{\infty})=\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\widetilde{\vec{\Psi}}_{\infty}),

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}\,\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,\infty}^{0}(\mathscr{L}_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}})\leq\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\chi}_{\infty})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\chi}_{\infty}).

(4.64)

Indeed, the function
\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
admits the following expansion as
|z|\rightarrow\infty

\displaystyle\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}(z)=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{A}_{0}z\right).

Therefore, the metric at infinity is flat, and (
4.62
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\Delta\psi_{\infty,j}\right)^{2}|dz|^{2}<\infty.

Since the metric on
\widetilde{C}=\mathbb{C}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}\simeq S^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle g_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}=\frac{4|dz|^{2}}{\left(1+|z|^{2}\right)^{2}},

we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}\left(1+|z|^{2}\right)^{2}\left(\Delta_{g_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}}\psi_{\infty,j}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}}<\infty.

Finally, if
u_{\infty,j}=|\vec{\chi}_{\infty}|^{2}\psi_{\infty,j}
, we have
u_{\infty,j}\in W^{2,2}(S^{2})
, and the previous formula (
4.63
) and the Lemma
4.5
show that (
4.64
) holds.
Notice that in higher codimension, if
\vec{\Psi}:\Sigma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\vec{\Psi}=\frac{\vec{\Phi}}{|\vec{\Phi}|^{2}}
, we have for all
\vec{v}\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma,\mathbb{R}^{n})
(see
[
41
]
)

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Psi}}(\vec{v})=Q_{\vec{\Phi}}\left(|\vec{\Phi}|^{2}\vec{v}-2\langle\vec{\Phi},\vec{v}\,\rangle\vec{\Phi}\right)

and the same proof applies.

Notice that
Claim 1
implies that
(\varphi_{\infty,j},\psi_{\infty,j})\neq(0,0)
. Now, assume by contradiction that

\displaystyle N=\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}N_{k}>\mathrm{dim}\,\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,\infty}^{0}+\mathrm{dim}\,\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,\infty}^{0}(\mathscr{L}_{g_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}}).

Then, we deduce that the family
(\varphi_{\infty,j},\psi_{\infty,j})_{1\leq j\leq N}
is linearly independent, which shows that there exists
(c_{\infty,1},\cdots,c_{\infty,N})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
(almost all
0
) such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j=1}^{N}c_{\infty,j}\,\varphi_{\infty,j}=0\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{N}c_{\infty,j}\,\psi_{\infty,j}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Finally, considering the variation
\displaystyle u_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}c_{\infty,j}\,\varphi_{k,j}
, we are led to a contradiction. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{k})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{k})\right)\leq\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{\infty})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{\infty})\right)+\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Psi}_{\infty})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Psi}_{\infty})\right)

and this concludes the proof of the main theorem, up to a last remark on non-compact bubbles.
∎

5
Improved Energy Quantization for Degenerating Riemann Surfaces

5.1
First Estimates on the Residues

In this section, we make more precise estimates from
[
25
]
and find a criterium under which
L^{2,1}
energy quantization holds, or more generally, is sufficiently small. This stronger quantization is necessary to get pointwise estimates of the second fundamental form in neck regions. First, recall that

\displaystyle d\vec{H}-3\pi_{\vec{n}}(d\vec{H})+\star(\vec{H}\wedge d\vec{n})

\displaystyle=-4\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(g^{-1}\otimes\left(\overline{\partial}^{N}-\overline{\partial}^{\top}\right)\vec{h}_{0}-|\vec{h}_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-4\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial\vec{H}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial\vec{\Phi}+\,g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}\right),

the four residues can be defined as (
[
42
]
)

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma}_{0}(\vec{\Phi},p)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{\gamma}\partial\vec{H}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial\vec{\Phi}+\,g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi},p)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{\gamma}\vec{\Phi}\wedge\left(\partial\vec{H}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial\vec{\Phi}+\,g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}\right)+g^{-1}\otimes\vec{h}_{0}\wedge\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}(\vec{\Phi},p)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{\gamma}\left(\partial\vec{H}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial\vec{\Phi}+\,g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}\right)\cdot\vec{\Phi}

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma}_{3}(\vec{\Phi},p)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{\gamma}\mathscr{I}_{\vec{\Phi}}\left(\partial\vec{H}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial\vec{\Phi}+\,g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}\right)-g^{-1}\otimes\left(\overline{\partial}|\vec{\Phi}|^{2}\otimes\vec{h}_{0}-2\langle\vec{\Phi},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}\right),

where
\mathscr{I}_{\vec{\Phi}}(\vec{X})=|\vec{\Phi}|^{2}\vec{X}-2\langle\vec{\Phi},\vec{X}\rangle\vec{\Phi}
, while
\gamma
is a closed simple curve around
p
. The residues are invariant under isometries,
\vec{\gamma}_{1}
is invariant under all conformal transformation, while an inversion changes the sign of
\gamma_{2}
and inverts
\vec{\gamma}_{0}
and
\vec{\gamma}_{4}
, the residues associated to the invariance under translations and inversions respectively.

If
\vec{\Phi}:\Omega=B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a weak conformal immersion, we have

\displaystyle\dive\left(\nabla\vec{H}-3\nabla^{N}\vec{H}+\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H})\right)=0\qquad\text{in}\;\,B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0).

Thanks to the Poincaré lemma, if

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma}_{0}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,\rho)}d\vec{H}-3\pi_{\vec{n}}(d\vec{H})+\star(\vec{H}\wedge d\vec{n})=-8\,\vec{\gamma}_{0}(\vec{\Phi},0)

(5.1)

for some
r\leq\rho\leq R
, then by the Poincaré lemma, there exists
\vec{L}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}\vec{L}=\nabla\vec{H}-3\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})+\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-\vec{\gamma}_{0}\nabla\log|z|

As in
[
25
]
, we have

\displaystyle\dive\left(\vec{L}\cdot\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}-\langle\vec{\gamma}_{0},\vec{\Phi}\rangle\nabla\log|z|\right)=0

\displaystyle\dive\left(\vec{L}\wedge\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}+2(-1)^{n-1}(\star(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\vec{H})\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}-\vec{\gamma}_{0}\wedge\vec{\Phi}\,\nabla\log|z|\right)=0.

Therefore, if

\displaystyle\widetilde{\vec{\gamma}_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,r)}\left(\vec{L}\wedge\partial_{\tau}\vec{\Phi}+2(-1)^{n-1}\left(\star(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\vec{H})\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\partial_{\tau}\vec{\Phi}\right)-\vec{\gamma}_{0}\wedge\vec{\Phi}\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,r)}\left(\langle\vec{L},\partial_{\tau}\vec{\Phi}\rangle+\langle\vec{\gamma_{0}},\vec{\Phi}\rangle\right)\,d\mathscr{H}^{1},

there exists
S:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\vec{R}:\Omega\rightarrow\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla\vec{R}&=\vec{L}\wedge\nabla\vec{\Phi}+2(-1)^{n-1}\left(\star(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\vec{H})\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}\right)+\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\nabla S&=\vec{L}\cdot\nabla\vec{\Phi}+\gamma_{2}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(5.2)

where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \vec{\gamma}_{1}&=\widetilde{\vec{\gamma}_{1}}+\vec{\gamma}_{0}\wedge\vec{\Phi}\\
\gamma_{2}&=\widetilde{\gamma_{2}}+\vec{\gamma}_{0}\cdot\vec{\Phi}\end{aligned}\right.

(5.3)

Using our precised
\varepsilon
-regularity result of Theorem
3.8
, we deduce that
[
25
, Lemma
4.1
]
can be more precisely stated as
follows.

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{1}(\Lambda)<\infty
such that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}
,

\displaystyle e^{\lambda(z)}|\vec{L}(z)|\leq\frac{C_{1}(\Lambda)}{|z|}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

The next lemma allows one to control the residue of
S
.

Lemma 5.2
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{2}(\Lambda)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\gamma_{2}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C_{2}(\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Proof.

Indeed, using the estimate just after equation (
28
) in
[
25
]
and our improved
\varepsilon
-regularity from Theorem
3.8
, the estimate follows immediately since
|\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(w)|\leq e^{\lambda}|z-w|
, and

\displaystyle|\gamma_{2}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(|z|\left\|\vec{\Phi}-\vec{\Phi}(z)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+|z|^{3}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))},

(5.4)

which concludes the proof of the lemma (notice that we used the Harnack inequality for the conformal parameter in this step). The conclusion follows from an generatisation of Lemma
4.3
from
[
25
]
that we state below.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
0<4\,r<R<\infty
and
\Omega=B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)
, and for all
0<t<1
, let
\Omega_{t}=B_{t\,R}\setminus\overline{B}_{t^{-1}\,r}(0)
. For all
2r<|z|<R/2
, and for all
f\in L^{2}(B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)
define
\delta(f):\Omega_{1/2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}

\displaystyle\delta(f)(z)=\left(\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}\int_{B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{|z|/2}(0)}|f|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then, there exists a universal constant independent
C<\infty
of
0<4\,r<R<\infty
such that for all
f,g\in L^{2}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\left\||z|\delta(f)\delta(g)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|g\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(5.5)

Proof.

Let
\varphi(r)=r\delta^{2}(f)(r)
. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\varphi=-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{A(2,r)}|f|^{2}dx+\frac{1}{r}\int_{\partial A(2,r)}|f|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{1}.

We have thanks to Fubini’s theorem

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left(\int_{A(2,x)}|f(y)|^{2}dy\right)dx=\int_{2r}^{R/2}\frac{1}{\rho}\left(\int_{B_{2\rho}\setminus\overline{B}_{\rho/2}(0)}|f(y)|^{2}dy\right)d\rho

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|f(y)|^{2}\left(\int_{2r}^{R/2}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\rho/2\leq|y|\leq 2\rho\right\}}}{\rho}d\rho\right)dy=\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|f(y)|^{2}\left(\int_{|y|/2}^{2|y|}\frac{d\rho}{\rho}\right)dy

\displaystyle=\log(4)\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|f(y)|^{2}dy.

(5.6)

Then, using the co-area formula (
[
13
,
\mathbf{3.2.11}
]
twice

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|x|}\left(\int_{\partial A(2,x)}|f(y)|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right)dx=\int_{2r}^{R/2}\left(\int_{\partial B(0,2\rho)}|f(y)|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right)d\rho

\displaystyle+\int_{2r}^{R/2}\left(\int_{\partial B(0,\rho/2)}|f(y)|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right)d\rho

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{4r}(0)}|f(x)|^{2}dx+\int_{B_{R/4}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|f(x)|^{2}dx\leq 2\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|f(x)|^{2}dx.

(5.7)

Therefore, since
\varphi
is a radial function, we deduce thanks to (
5.1
) and (
5.1
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}|\nabla\varphi|dx\leq\left(2+\log(4)\right)\int_{\Omega}|f(x)|^{2}dx.

(5.8)

Furthermore, using Fubini’s theorem and the co-area formula, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\varphi|dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|x|}\left(\int_{A(2,x)}|f(y)|^{2}dy\right)dx=\int_{2r}^{R/2}\left(\int_{B_{2\rho}\setminus\overline{B}_{\rho/2}(0)}|f(y)|^{2}dy\right)d\rho

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|f(y)|^{2}\left(\int_{|y|/2}^{2|y|}d\rho\right)dy=\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}|y||f(y)|^{2}dy.

(5.9)

Now, thanks to the improved Sobolev embedding
W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we deduce by a trivial scaling argument that there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{1,1}(B(0,R))

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,R))}\leq C_{1}\left(\int_{B(0,R)}|\nabla u|dx+\frac{1}{R}\int_{B(0,R)}|u|dx\right).

(5.10)

for some universal constant
C_{2}<\infty
.
Furthermore, since the conformal class of
\Omega
is bounded away from
-\infty
, using
[
46
, Lemma
7.2
]
, we deduce that there exists an extension operator
T:W^{1,1}(\Omega)\rightarrow W^{1,1}(B(0,R))
and a universal constant independent of
0<4\,r<R<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{1,1}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\left\|Tu\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,1}(B(0,R))}\leq C_{3}\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,1}(\Omega)}.

(5.11)

Combining (
5.11
) and (
5.10
) we deduce that there exists a universal constant
C_{4}<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{1,1}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\leq C_{4}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|dx+\frac{1}{R}\int_{\Omega}|u|dx\right).

(5.12)

Gathering the estimates (
5.8
), (
5.1
), and (
5.12
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\varphi|dx+\frac{1}{R}\int_{\Omega}|\varphi|dx\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\left((2+\log(4))\int_{\Omega}|f(x)|^{2}dx+2\int_{\Omega}\frac{|x|}{R}|f(x)|^{2}dx\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\left(4+\log(4)\right)\int_{\Omega}|f(x)|^{2}dx.

(5.13)

In other words

\displaystyle\left\||z|\delta^{2}(f)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

(5.14)

Then, using the Hölder’s inequality for Lorentz spaces, we deduce by (
5.14
) that

\displaystyle\left\||z|\delta(f)\delta(g)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{H}\left\||z|^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta(f)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\left\||z|^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta(g)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{H}^{\prime}\left\||z|\delta^{2}(f)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\||z|\delta^{2}(g)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{H}^{\prime}C_{4}(4+\log(4))\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\left\|g\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})},

(5.15)

where the constants
C_{H}
and
C_{H}^{\prime}
are independent of the domain, since the inequalities are verified for a general measured space
(X,\mu)
We also used the fact that the
L^{p,q}
norm is equivalent to the following semi-norm:

\displaystyle|f|_{\mathrm{L}^{p,q}(X)}=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{q}\lambda_{f}(t)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},

where
\lambda_{f}(t)=\mu\left(X\cap\left\{x:|f(x)|>t\right\}\right)
for all
t>0
. Noticing that
\lambda_{\sqrt{|f|}}(t)=\lambda_{f}(t^{2})
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{|f|}\right|_{\mathrm{L}^{p,q}(X)}=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{q}\lambda_{f}(t^{2})^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\frac{q}{2}}\lambda_{f}(s)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{ds}{2s}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}=\frac{1}{2^{\frac{1}{q}}}\left|f\right|_{\mathrm{L}^{\frac{p}{2},\frac{q}{2}}(X)}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.16)

This remark closes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now, we make more precise
[
25
, Lemma
4.4
]
.

Lemma 5.4
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{3}(\Lambda)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C_{3}(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(5.17)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\left(\left(\pi_{T}\left(\partial_{r}\vec{H}\right)-2\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(\partial_{r}\vec{H}\right)-\star\left(\partial_{\tau}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\wedge(\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(y))\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2(-1)^{m}\left(\star\left(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\vec{H}\right)\right)\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\partial_{\tau}\vec{\Phi}\right)\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y),

that we estimate as in
[
25
]
as

\displaystyle|\vec{\gamma}_{1}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(|z|+|z|^{2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))},

(5.18)

which shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\log(4)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(5.19)

and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.2
Finer Estimates of Residues

Now, recall that the following system holds true:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla\vec{R}&=(-1)^{n}\star\left(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}\right)+\star\,\vec{n}\cdot\nabla^{\perp}S+\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|\\
&+(-1)^{n}\star\left(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\vec{\gamma}_{1}\right)\nabla\log|z|+\gamma_{2}\nabla\log|z|\star\vec{n}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\nabla S&=-\star\vec{n}\cdot\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}+\gamma_{2}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|-\star\,\vec{n}\cdot\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla\log|z|\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\end{aligned}\right.

(5.20)

Thanks to the previous analysis, since
\gamma_{2}\nabla\log|z|\in L^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})
while
\vec{\gamma_{1}}\nabla\log|z|\in L^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})
, we need to prove finer estimates for algebraic components featuring
\vec{\gamma}_{1}
.

Furthermore, the following algebraic identity is verified:

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda}\vec{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{\perp}S\cdot\nabla\vec{\Phi}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla\vec{R}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{\gamma_{2}}{|z|}\partial_{r}\vec{\Phi}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{\vec{\gamma}_{1}}{|z|}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\partial_{r}\vec{\Phi}.

(5.21)

First, let us make precise
[
25
, Lemma
5.1
]
.

Lemma 5.5
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{4}(\Lambda)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\star\,\vec{n}\cdot\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C_{4}(\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\left(\pi_{T}\left(\partial_{r}\vec{H}\right)-2\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{r}\vec{H})-\star\left(\partial_{\tau}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\wedge(\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(y))d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{n}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\left(\star(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\vec{H})\right)\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\partial_{\tau}\vec{\Phi}\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}.

(5.22)

Notice that in a conformal chart, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{T}(\partial_{z}\vec{H})

\displaystyle=2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\partial_{z}\vec{H},\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\rangle\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}+2e^{-2\lambda}\langle\partial_{z}\vec{H},\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}

\displaystyle=-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}\vec{\Phi}\rangle\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}-2\,e^{-2\lambda}\langle\vec{H},\partial_{z}^{2}\vec{\Phi}\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}

\displaystyle=-|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}-\langle\vec{H},\vec{H}_{0}\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle|\pi_{T}(\nabla\vec{H})|\leq\left(e^{\lambda}|\vec{H}|+e^{\lambda}|\vec{H}_{0}|\right)|\vec{H}|\leq 2|\nabla\vec{n}||\vec{H}|.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\pi_{T}\left(\partial_{r}\vec{H}\right)\wedge\left(\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(y)\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|z|^{2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(5.23)

A fortiori
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\star\,\vec{n}(z)\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\pi_{T}\left(\partial_{r}\vec{H}\right)\wedge\left(\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(y)\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right|\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(5.24)

On the other hand, we directly estimate

\displaystyle\left|\star\vec{n}(z)\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi}\star\left(\partial_{\tau}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\wedge(\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(y))d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|z|^{2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(5.25)

Since
\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{r}\vec{H})
is a normal vector, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\langle\star\,\vec{n},\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{r}\vec{H})\wedge\vec{v}\right\rangle=\left\langle\vec{e}_{1}\wedge\vec{e}_{2},\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{r}\vec{H})\wedge\vec{v}\right\rangle=0\quad\text{for all}\;\,\vec{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(5.26)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left|\star\,\vec{n}(z)\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(\partial_{r}\vec{H}\right)\wedge(\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(y))d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left|\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\left(\star\,\vec{n}(z)-\star\,\vec{n}(y)\right)\cdot\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{r}\vec{H})\wedge\left(\vec{\Phi}(z)-\vec{\Phi}(y)\right)\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|z|^{3}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\nabla\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}

(5.27)

where we used once more the improved
\varepsilon
-regularity from Theorem
3.8
together with the classical
\varepsilon
-regularity from
[
55
]
. Finally, noticing as in (
5.26
) holds for
\vec{H}\wedge\partial_{x_{i}}\vec{\Phi}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\star\,\vec{n}(z)\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\left(\star\left(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\vec{H}\right)\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\partial_{\tau}\vec{\Phi}\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left|\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\left(\star\,\vec{n}(z)-\star\,\vec{n}(y)\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\vec{\Phi}\wedge\vec{H}\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|z|^{2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(5.28)

Gathering estimates (
5.24
), (
5.2
), (
5.2
), and (
5.2
), we deduce by the algebraic identity (
5.2
) that

\displaystyle\left|\star\,\vec{n}(z)\cdot\vec{\gamma}_{1}(z)\right|\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}.

(5.29)

Therefore, the proof is completed by virtue of Lemma (
5.3
).
∎

The next lemma is straightforward (see
[
25
, Lemma
5.2
]
.

Lemma 5.6
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{5}(\Lambda)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{\gamma}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C_{5}(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Proof.

Recall by (
5.1
) that

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma_{0}}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial B(0,\rho)}d\vec{H}-3\pi_{\vec{n}}(d\vec{H})+\star(\vec{H}\wedge d\vec{n}).

Since
\vec{\gamma}_{1}(z)=\widetilde{\gamma_{1}}+\vec{\gamma}_{0}\wedge\vec{\Phi}(z)
, we have
\nabla\vec{\gamma}_{1}=\vec{\gamma}\wedge\nabla\vec{\Phi}
, which implies that

\displaystyle|\nabla\vec{\gamma}_{1}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq C|z|\left(\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}+\left\|e^{\lambda}|\vec{H}||\nabla\vec{n}|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|z|}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\delta(z),

(5.30)

which shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{\gamma}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C\sqrt{\log(4)}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

and concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

We skip the proof of the next estimate that is immediately derived from the second inequality in the proof of
[
25
, Lemma
5.3
]

Lemma 5.7
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{6}(\Lambda)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle|\Delta\vec{\gamma}_{1}(z)|\leq\frac{C_{6}(\Lambda)}{|z|^{2}}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}=C_{6}(\Lambda)\delta_{0}(z)\,\delta(z).

(5.31)

Then, the follow result holds.

Lemma 5.8
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{7}(\Lambda)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{\gamma}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C_{7}(\Lambda)\left(R+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\sup_{2r<\rho<R/2}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,\rho))}.

Proof.

Integrating by parts and using (
5.1
), (
5.2
), and (
5.31
), we obtain thanks to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}|\nabla\vec{\gamma}_{1}|^{2}dx=-\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\vec{\gamma}_{1}\cdot\Delta\vec{\gamma}_{1}\,dx+\int_{\partial\Omega_{1/2}}\vec{\gamma}_{1}\cdot\partial_{\nu}\vec{\gamma}_{1}\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}|dz|^{2}+C\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|z|}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left(\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}|dz|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}\left\|e^{2\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}|dz|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}|dz|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\pi(R^{2}-r^{2})}\right)\sup_{2r<\rho<R/2}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,\rho))}

\displaystyle=C\left(\log(4)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\sqrt{\log(4)}\sqrt{\pi(R^{2}-r^{2})}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\sup_{2r<\rho<R/2}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,\rho))}.

Notice that we also estimate directly

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}|dz|^{2}+\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\frac{1}{|z|}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}^{2}|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\log(4)\sup_{2r<\rho<R/2}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,z))}\int_{\Omega}e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}|x|e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx.

∎

5.3
Estimates on the Jacobian System

5.3.1
Marcinkiewicz Space Estimate

Recalling system (
5.2
)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla\vec{R}&=\vec{L}\wedge\nabla\vec{\Phi}+2(-1)^{n-1}\left(\star(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\vec{H})\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}\right)+\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\nabla S&=\vec{L}\cdot\nabla\vec{\Phi}+\gamma_{2}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

(5.32)

we deduce thanks to Lemma
5.1
that for all
z\in\Omega_{1/2}

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} |\nabla\vec{R}(z)|&\leq 2e^{\lambda(z)}|\vec{L}(z)|+4e^{\lambda(z)}|\vec{H}(z)|+\frac{|\gamma_{1}(z)|}{|z|}\leq\frac{C(\Lambda)}{|z|}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{|\gamma_{1}(z)|}{|z|}\\
|\nabla S(z)|&\leq 2e^{\lambda}|\vec{L}(z)|+\frac{|\gamma_{2}(z)|}{|z|}\leq\frac{C(\Lambda)}{|z|}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{|\gamma_{2}(z)|}{|z|}\end{aligned}\right.

(5.33)

where we used the
\varepsilon
-regularity from Theorem
3.8
. Thanks to Lemma
5.1
Lemma
5.2
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{L}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}+2\left\|\gamma_{2}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\pi}C_{1}(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+2C_{2}(\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(5.34)

while Lemma
5.1
and Lemma
5.4
shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(5.35)

5.3.2
Lebesgue Space Estimate

Using the same argument as in
[
25
]
, and the pointwise estimates (
5.1
) and (
5.1
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\vec{R}}{d\rho}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{dS_{\rho}}{d\rho}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\rho^{2}}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,\rho))}

(5.36)

where for all
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
0<\rho<\infty
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\rho}={\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int}_{\mkern-10.0mu\partial B(0,\rho)}\varphi\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\left(|\nabla\vec{R}_{|z|}|^{2}+|\nabla S_{|z|}|^{2}\right)|dz|^{2}=\int_{2r}^{R/2}\left(\left|\frac{d\vec{R}}{d\rho}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{dS_{\rho}}{d\rho}\right|^{2}\right)\rho\,d\rho\leq C\int_{\Omega}e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx.

(5.37)

Now, let
\vec{\psi}_{\vec{R}}:\Omega\rightarrow\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the solution of

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{\psi}_{\vec{R}}&=\dive\left(\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|+(-1)^{n}\star\left(\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\vec{\gamma}_{1})\right)\nabla\log|z|+\gamma_{2}\nabla\log|z|\star\vec{n}\right)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega_{1/2}\\
\vec{\psi}_{\vec{R}}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega_{1/2},\end{aligned}\right.

(5.38)

and
\psi_{S}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the solution of

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta{\psi}_{\vec{R}}&=\dive\left(\gamma_{2}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|-\star\,\vec{n}\cdot\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla\log|z|\right)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega_{1/2}\\
{\psi}_{\vec{R}}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega_{1/2}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.39)

Integrating by parts, we deduce that thanks to Lemma
5.2
and (
5.4
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}|\nabla\psi_{S}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\psi_{S}\Delta\psi_{S}\,dx=\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}\nabla\psi_{S}\cdot\left(\gamma_{2}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|+\star\,\vec{n}\cdot\vec{\gamma_{1}}\nabla\log|z|\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}|\nabla\psi_{S}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left\|\gamma_{2}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\left\|\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}|\nabla\psi_{S}|^{2}dx\right)\left(C_{2}(\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+C_{3}(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\Lambda)\left(\int_{\Omega_{1/2}}|\nabla\psi_{S}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\psi_{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Likewise,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\psi_{\vec{R}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Therefore, we finally deduce thanks to
[
46
, Lemma
5.5
]

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/4})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla S_{|z|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}+\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{1/2})}\right)+\left\|\nabla\psi_{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(5.40)

Likewise, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{1/4})}\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(5.41)

5.3.3
Lorentz Space Estimate

Using the same argument as in
[
55
, (
60
)]
and (
5.29
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{dS_{\rho}}{d\rho}\right|\leq\left(\int_{\partial B(0,\rho)}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\partial B(0,\rho)}|\nabla\vec{R}|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{C}{|z|}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,\rho))}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A(2,\rho))}.

Therefore, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the co-area formula, we deduce thanks to (
5.41
) that

\displaystyle\int_{8r}^{R/8}\left|\frac{dS_{\rho}}{d\rho}\right|d\rho\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

and finally

\displaystyle\left\|S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1/8})}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Reasoning similarly for
\vec{R}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1/8})}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Finally, using
[
25
, Lemma
5.6
]
(or rather, the more precise
[
46
, Lemma
4.5
]
), and the
L^{2,1}
estimates from Lemma
5.2
and Lemma
5.5
that

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1/8})}\leq C\left\|\nabla\psi_{S}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/8})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\gamma_{2}\nabla^{\perp}\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/8})}+\left\|\star\,\vec{n}\cdot\vec{\gamma}_{1}\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Finally, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/8})}\leq C(\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

As in
[
25
]
, the
L^{2,1}
estimate does not hold for
\vec{R}
. However, following the same steps of the proof, we deduce thanks to (
5.2
)

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/2})}\leq C(\Lambda)+|\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi},0)|\left\|\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{1/8})}

As

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{e^{-l_{k}}}(0))}=4\sqrt{\pi}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{e^{-l_{k}}}\right)+\log\left(1+\sqrt{1-e^{-2l_{k}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=4\sqrt{\pi}\left(l_{k}+\log\left(1+\sqrt{1-e^{-2l_{k}}}\right)\right)=4\sqrt{\pi}\left(l_{k}+O\left(\sqrt{l_{k}}\right)\right)=4\sqrt{\pi}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{l_{k}}}\right)\right)l_{k},

where
l_{k}\rightarrow\infty
, we deduce that the
L^{2,1}
quantization holds if

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}|\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi}_{k})|l_{k}=0.

6
Proof of the Main Theorem in the General Setting

The goal of this setion is to show the following result. Recall that for all Willmore immersion
\vec{\Phi}
, the second residue is defined by

\displaystyle\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi},p)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{\gamma}\vec{\Phi}\wedge\left(\partial\vec{H}+|\vec{H}|^{2}\partial\vec{\Phi}+g^{-1}\otimes\langle\vec{H},\vec{h}_{0}\rangle\otimes\overline{\partial\vec{\Phi}}\right)+g^{-1}\otimes\vec{h}_{0}\wedge\overline{\partial}\vec{\Phi}.

Theorem 6.1
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a universal constant
\Lambda_{n}>0
with the following property.
Let
(\Sigma,h_{k})
be sequence of compact Riemann surfaces of fixed genus that converges to a punctured
(\overline{\Sigma},\overline{h})
whose connected components are
(\overline{\Sigma}_{1},\cdots,\overline{\Sigma}_{p})
, while we denote by
\left\{\gamma_{k}^{1},\cdots\gamma_{k}^{q}\right\}
the finite sequence of pinching geodesics. For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\vec{\Phi}_{k}:(\Sigma,h_{k})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a sequence of smooth Willmore immersions such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}W(\vec{\Phi}_{k})<\infty,

(6.1)

and assume that for all
1\leq j\leq q
, if
l_{k}^{j}=\mathscr{L}(\gamma_{k}^{j})
is the length of
\gamma_{j}^{j}
, the

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{|\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi}_{k},\gamma_{k}^{j})|}{l_{k}^{j}}\leq\Lambda_{n},

(6.2)

then there exists branched Willmore immersions
\vec{\Phi}_{\infty}^{i}:\bar{\Sigma}_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
and branched Willmore spheres
\vec{\Psi}_{1},\cdots\vec{\Psi}_{r}:S^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
\vec{\chi}_{1},\cdots\vec{\chi}_{s}:S^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{k})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{\infty}^{i})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{\infty}^{i})\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Psi}_{j})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\Psi}_{j})\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{s}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\chi}_{l})+\mathrm{Null}_{W}(\vec{\chi}_{l})\right).

(6.3)

Remark 6.2
.

Notice that the
L^{2}
energy quantization holds since (
6.2
) implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi}_{k},\gamma_{k}^{j})}{\sqrt{l_{k}^{j}}}=0,

which allows us to use the analysis of
[
26
]
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as the one given in Section
4
with the exception of Theorem
4.1
that must be refined. The previous analysis shows the result holds provided that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}

is small enough, and since

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{2}(2\alpha))}+|\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi}_{k},0)|\left\|\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}\right).

Since

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\log|z|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}=4\sqrt{\pi}\left(l_{k}+2\log(\alpha)+\log\left(1+\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}e^{-2l_{k}}}\right)\right),

where
l_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\infty

we deduce thanks to the
L^{2}
quantization of energy that for all
\varepsilon>0
, provided that

\displaystyle|\vec{\gamma}_{1}(\vec{\Phi}_{k},0)|l_{k}\leq\varepsilon,

then

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}\leq 4\sqrt{\pi}C\varepsilon.

Therefore, using (
4
), we deduce that the theorem holds provided that
\varepsilon>0
is chosen small enough in the above estimate, which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎
